PN CN120157621-A
TI Synthesizing acetamide compound using visible            light-induced defluorination coupling comprises e.g.            adding eneamide, trifluoromethylbenzimidazole,            photocatalyst fac-tris (2-phenylpyridine), cesium            formate and ultra-dry nitrogen-nitrogen            dimethylformamide into reaction tube, extracting and            spin-drying.
AU SHEN K
   HUANG J
   ZHAO K
AE UNIV NANJING TECHNOLOGY (UNTY-C)
AB    NOVELTY - Synthesizing acetamide compound (III) using                visible light-induced defluorination coupling                comprises (i) adding eneamide (I),                trifluoromethylbenzimidazole (II), photocatalyst                fac-tris (2-phenylpyridine), cesium formate and                ultra-dry nitrogen-nitrogen dimethylformamide into                10 ml reaction tube and placing in a nitrogen                atmosphere using 30 W as visible light source, (ii)                when the raw material enamide is completely                consumed, stopping the reaction under the                irradiation of visible light, and (iii) extracting                the reaction product for three times in a                separatory funnel to remove ultra-dry                nitrogen-nitrogen dimethylformamide, spin-drying at                low pressure on a rotary evaporator, and separating                crude product by column chromatography to obtain                acetamide compound.
   USE - The method is useful in synthesizing acetamide                compound using visible light-induced defluorination                coupling (claimed).
   ADVANTAGE - The method: has mild reaction                conditions.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Synthesizing acetamide compound of formula                (III) using visible light-induced defluorination                coupling comprises (i) adding eneamide of formula                (I), trifluoromethylbenzimidazole of formula (II),                photocatalyst fac-tris (2-phenylpyridine), cesium                formate and ultra-dry nitrogen-nitrogen                dimethylformamide into 10 ml reaction tube and                placing in a nitrogen atmosphere using 30 W as                visible light source, (ii) when the raw material                enamide is completely consumed, stopping the                reaction under the irradiation of visible light,                and (iii) extracting the reaction product for three                times in a separatory funnel to remove ultra-dry                nitrogen-nitrogen dimethylformamide, spin-drying at                low pressure on a rotary evaporator, and separating                crude product by column chromatography to obtain                acetamide compound.R = H, 4-fluoro, 4-chloro,                4-bromo, 3-bromo, 3-bromo, 4-fluoro, 2-chloro,                2-fluoro, 2,4-dichloro or other groups.
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The enamide derivative                  is acetyl enamide. The acetamido enamide is                  N-(1-phenylvinyl) acetamide or its derivative.                  The trifluoromethylbenzimidazole is                  1-methyl-2-(trifluoromethyl)-1H-naphtho[2,3-d]imidazole.                  The reaction molar ratio between raw material                  (I), raw material (II), photocatalyst fac-tris                  (2-phenylpyridine) and cesium formate is                  1:2:0.01:3. The selected solvent is ultra-dry                  nitrogen-nitrogen dimethylformamide.Preferred Method: The reaction is carried                  out at room temperature for 24 hours.

PN EP4567124-A1; WO2025120052-A2; WO2025120052-A3
TI Producing 4-hydroxymethylfurfural involves            converting 4-hydroxymethylfurfural-phosphate to            4-hydroxmethylfurfural using a phosphatase.
AU PICK A
AE CASCAT GMBH (CASC-Non-standard)
   CASCAT GMBH (CASC-Non-standard)
AB    NOVELTY - Producing 4-hydroxymethylfurfural (HMF)                involves converting                4-hydroxymethylfurfural-phosphate to                4-hydroxmethylfurfural using a phosphatase.
   USE - Method for producing 4-hydroxymethylfurfural                (HMF) by using phosphatase (claimed).
   ADVANTAGE - The method is cell-free, one-pot reaction                and/or is portion of multi-enzyme reaction cascade,                reduces production of unwanted side-product which                makes purification straight forward and cheaper,                reduces the cost by adding phosphatase, and                recycles the phosphate in the process.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are included for:
   a method for producing 4-HMF from a feedstock                comprising a carbon source by converting                4-hydroxymethylfurfural-phosphate to                4-hydroxmethylfurfural using a phosphatase, where                carbon source is preferably selected from a group                of starch, dextrines, cellulose, sucrose and/or its                combination; and
   a phosphatase for converting                4-hydroxymethylfurfural-phosphate to                4-hydroxmethylfurfural, which comprises amino acid                sequence that is at least 95-99% identical with                amino acid sequence (SEQ ID NO: 51), where                sequences are not defined.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - BIOTECHNOLOGY - Preferred Conditions: The phosphatase is                  obtained from an organism, preferably a                  microorganism, and more preferably from a                  microorganism selected from a group of                  Escherichia coli and/or Blautia obeum; and/or                  phosphatase has an activity towards other                  compounds present in process being less than or                  equal to 10% as compared to activity towards                  4-hydroxymethylfurfural-phosphate; and/or                  conversion of 4-hydroxymethylfurfural-phosphate                  to 4-hydroxmethylfurfural is an irreversible                  reaction. The phosphate created in the conversion                  from 4-hydroxymethylfurfural-phosphate to                  4-hydroxmethylfurfural is recycled, where NAD                  &lt; + &gt; /NADH, NADP &lt; +                  &gt; /NADPH, Flavin Adenine Dinucleotide                  (FAD), Flavin Mononucleotide (FMN) or ATP are not                  needed in the conversion from                  4-hydroxymethylfurfural-phosphate to                  4-hydroxmethylfurfural no co-factor. The                  4-hydroxymethylfurfural-phosphate is prepared                  from glyceraldehyde-3-phosphate using at least                  one enzyme; or 4-hydroxymethylfurfural-phosphate                  is prepared from glyceraldehyde-3-phosphate using                  at least one enzyme and phosphate created in the                  conversion is recycled in the process. The enzyme                  is a synthase, and synthase is obtained from an                  organism, preferably a microorganism selected                  from a group of Methanocaldococcus jannaschii,                  Methanococcus maripaludis, Methanothermococcus                  okinawensis, Methanobacterium paludism,                  Methanobrevibacter wolinii, Methylomonas                  methanica, Methanosarcina mazei, and                  Methanothermococcus thermolithotrophicus. The                  glyceraldehyde-3-phosphate is prepared from                  fructose-6-phosphate using at least one enzyme,                  where reaction yields dihydroxyacetone in a                  non-phosphorylated form; or                  glyceraldehyde-3-phosphate is prepared from                  fructose-6-phosphate using at least one enzyme,                  where reaction yields dihydroxyacetone in a                  non-phosphorylated form, and dihydroxyacetone is                  isomerized to glyceraldehyde using an isomerase,                  where isomerase is an isomerase belonging to the                  EC5.3.1 class, preferably isomerase is an                  isomerase belonging to the EC5.3.1.22 class or                  EC5.3.1.35 class. The dihydroxyacetone is further                  converted into fructose, preferably the mixture                  of dihydroxyacetone and glyceraldehyde is                  converted into fructose. The synthase comprises                  an amino acid sequence that is at least 80-99%                  identical with amino acid sequence (SEQ ID NO:                  23), and synthase has amino acid sequence (SEQ ID                  NOS: 15, 17, 19, 21, 25, 27, or 29). The                  phosphatase comprises an amino acid sequence that                  is at least 95-99% identical with amino acid                  sequence (SEQ ID NO: 51), preferably the                  phosphatase is derived from Escherichia coli; or                  phosphatase comprises an amino acid sequence that                  is at least 80-99% identical with amino acid                  sequence (SEQ ID NO: 35), preferably the                  phosphatase is derived from a Blautia obeum. The                  process is a multi-enzyme reaction cascade,                  and/or process comprises at least five enzymes,                  where phosphate created in the production of                  4-hydroxmethylfurfural is recycled in the                  production process. The phosphatase comprises                  amino acid sequence (SEQ ID NOS: 35, 37, 47, 49,                  51, or 53), where sequences are not                  defined.
   EXAMPLE - None given.

PN CN119741995-A; CN119741995-B
TI Processing method for predicting atomic level            reaction site, involves performing            electrophilic/nucleophilic reaction site atomic            information summary to obtain summary information to            feedback to client according to molecular structure and            prediction tensor.
AU ZHANG L
   KE G
   GAO Z
   ZHAO G
AE BEIJING DP TECHNOLOGY CO LTD (BEIJ-Non-standard)
AB    NOVELTY - The method involves constructing a processing                model for predicting an electrophilic/nucleophilic                reaction site of an atom level of a                three-dimensional (3D) molecular structure as a                corresponding reaction site prediction model. An                original data set is constructed. A model training                data set is constructed based on the original data                set, a preset quantum chemical calculation tool,                and an experiment means to obtain a data set. The                prediction model is trained based on the data set.                A 3D molecular structure of any compound molecule                input is received as a current 3D molecular                structure by a client. The molecular structure is                inputted into the model to perform atomic-level                electrophile/nucleophile reaction site prediction                processing to obtain a current reaction site                prediction tensor. Electrophilic/nucleophilic                reaction site atomic information summary is                performed to obtain summary information to feedback                to the client according to the molecular structure                and the prediction tensor.
   USE - Processing method for predicting atomic level                reaction site.
   ADVANTAGE - The method enables improving the prediction                efficiency by a reaction site prediction                model.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are included for: (1) a                device for performing a processing method for                atomic level reaction site prediction; (2) an                electronic device; (3) a computer-readable storage                medium storing a set of instructions for predicting                atomic level reaction site.
   DESCRIPTION OF DRAWING(S) - The drawing shows a flow diagram illustrating                a processing method for predicting atomic level                reaction site. (Drawing includes non-English                language text).

TI Copper-based catalyst useful in hydrogenation            reaction of furfural and furfuryl alcohol as substrate,            comprises copper, and chromium, aluminum, zinc,            magnesium, zirconium, and/or silicon.
AU ZHENG C
   SI Y
   ZHAO W
   LIU L
   CHENG Y
AE UNIV ZHEJIANG (UYZH-C)
AB    NOVELTY - Copper-based catalyst, comprises an active                component and an auxiliary component; where the                active component is copper, and the auxiliary                component includes chromium, aluminum, zinc,                magnesium, zirconium, and/or silicon.
   USE - The catalyst is useful in hydrogenation                reaction of furfural and furfuryl alcohol as                substrate (claimed).
   ADVANTAGE - The catalyst preparation method is simple,                low-cost and has good cycle stability. The                catalytic reaction system is green and                environmentally friendly, the reaction substrate is                renewable, the reaction conditions are mild, the                comprehensive yield of cyclopentanone is more than                90% under experimental conditions, and the catalyst                has low production cost and has broad market                application prospects.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are also included                for:
   preparing the catalyst, comprising dropping                the aqueous solution of the precipitant into the                metal salt aqueous solution, obtaining the mixture                after fully reacting, filtering, washing, drying,                calcining and reducing to obtain the composite                catalyst; or adding silica to the metal salt                aqueous solution, and then dropping the aqueous                solution of the precipitant, reacting sufficiently                to obtain the mixture, and filtering, washing,                drying, calcining and reducing to obtain the                copper/silica catalyst; and
   preparing cyclopentanone by furfural catalytic                hydrogenation rearrangement, comprising adding                furfural, solvent and the copper-based catalyst in                the high-pressure reaction device, performing                catalytic selective hydrogenation reaction in                hydrogen while stirring to obtain the product                cyclopentanone.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - INORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The auxiliary                  component is silicon, the copper-based catalyst                  is a copper/silica catalyst; when the auxiliary                  component is chromium, aluminum, zinc, magnesium,                  and/or zirconium; the copper-based catalyst is a                  composite catalyst, and the active components and                  auxiliary components of the composite catalyst                  are respectively derived from the corresponding                  metal salt solution. The metal salt of the active                  component of the catalyst comprises cupric                  nitrate trihydrate, cupric chloride dihydrate,                  cupric sulfate pentahydrate, preferably cupric                  nitrate trihydrate; the metal salt of the                  auxiliary component is zinc nitrate hexahydrate,                  zinc sulfate monohydrate, zinc acetate dihydrate;                  zirconia dinitrate hydrate, zirconium oxychloride                  hydrate; chromium nitrate nonohydrate, chromium                  chloride hexahydrate; aluminum nitrate                  hexahydrate, aluminum chloride hexahydrate;                  magnesium nitrate hexahydrate, magnesium chloride                  hexahydrate, preferably zinc nitrate hexahydrate                  and/or zirconia dinitrate hydrate. The molar                  ratio of the auxiliary component to copper in the                  composite catalyst is: (0.1-1.5):1, preferably                  0.5-1.0:1; the precipitant comprises sodium                  carbonate, potassium carbonate, sodium hydroxide,                  potassium hydroxide, aqueous ammonia, and/or                  urea; and in terms of molar amount, the                  precipitant: (active component + auxiliary                  component) is: 1.1-3.0:1, preferably 1.4-2:1. The                  amount of the copper-based catalyst is 1/10 to 1,                  preferably 1/2, of the mass of furfural, the                  initial hydrogen pressure is 2-6MPa, the reaction                  temperature is 140-220&#8451;, preferably                  160-180&#8451;, and the reaction time is 1-4                  hours, preferably 2-5 hours; and the solvent is a                  mixed solvent of deionized water and low-carbon                  alcohol.Preferred Method: The reaction temperature                  after adding the precipitant is 30-90&#8451;,                  preferably 60&#8451;; the reaction time is                  3-9 hours, preferably 6 hours; the drying is                  performed at 85-95&#8451; for 8-12 hours; the                  calcination is performed at 300-600&#8451;                  for 2-6 hours; the reduction is performed at                  300-600&#8451; with 5-15% hydrogen/argon for                  2-6 hours, preferably at 450&#8451; with                  5-15% hydrogen/argon for 4 hours.
   TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The low-carbon alcohol                  in the mixed solvent comprises methanol, ethanol,                  isopropanol, n-propanol, and/or tert-butanol,                  preferably isopropanol; and the mass ratio of                  water to alcohol in the mixed solvent is                  1-10:1.

AB    NOVELTY - Preparation of methyl                2-acetamido-2-(tert-butyl-carbamoyl)-dioxaborolanyl                cyclohexane carboxylate (I), involves (a) reacting                (S)-2-(1,4-dioxaspiro[4.5]decan-7-yl)ethan-1-ol                (II) with e.g. bromine, washing, and evaporating to                provide                (R)-7-(2-bromoethyl)-1,4-dioxaspiro[4.5]decane                (III), (b) reacting the compound (III) with                hydrochloric acid, washing, and evaporating to                provide crude (R)-3-(2-bromoethyl)cyclohexan-1-one                (IV), (c) reacting the compound (IV) with mixture                of e.g. triphenylphosphine, separating, and                distilling to provide                (R)-3-(2-(4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1,3,2-dioxaborolan-2-yl)ethyl)-cyclohexan-1-one                (V), (d) reacting the compound (V) with dimethyl                carbonate, and extraction to provide methyl                (4R)-2-oxo-4-(2-(4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1,3,2-dioxaborolan-2-yl)ethyl)cyclohexane-1-carboxylate                (VI), (e) dissolving the compound (VI) in a                solvent, adding ammonium acetate, cooling, and                treating with tert-butyl isocyanide to afford the                crude compound of the compound (I), and (f)                purifying.
   USE - Preparation of methyl                (1A,2A,4A)-2-acetamido-2-(tert-butyl-carbamoyl)-4-(2-(4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1,3,2-dioxaborolan-2-yl)ethyl)cyclohexane-1-carboxylate                used for preparing solid crystalline                (1A,2S,5R)-1-amino-5-[2-(dihydroxyboranyl)ethyl]-2-[(dimethylamino)methyl]cyclohexane-1-carboxylic                acid in anhydrous form A (all claimed) for treating                disorder, disease, or condition such as                cardiovascular disorder, sexual disorder, wound                healing disorder, gastrointestinal disorder,                autoimmune disorder, immune disorder, human                immunodeficiency virus (HIV) infection, hepatitis B                virus (HBV) infection, hepatitis C virus (HCV)                infection, Helicobacter pylori infections,                pulmonary disorder, hemolytic disorder, and                neoplastic disease.    ADVANTAGE - The method avoids using heavy metal based                catalysts, performs only one filtration through the                silica gel column chromatography, and provides                final product in a repeatable manner in terms of                the polymorphic form, purity and chiral purity,                which meets high standards for drug                substance.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Preparation of methyl                (1A,2A,4A)-2-acetamido-2-(tert-butyl-carbamoyl)-4-(2-(4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1,3,2-dioxaborolan-2-yl)ethyl)cyclohexane-1-carboxylate                of formula (I), involves (a) reacting                (S)-2-(1,4-dioxaspiro[4.5]decan-7-yl)ethan-1-ol of                formula (II) with bromine and triphenylphosphine in                the presence of an organic base in a reaction                solvent at room temperature, quenching the reaction                mixture and washing with water, saturated aqueous                sodium bicarbonate, and aqueous sodium thiosulfate,                precipitating triphenylphosphine using a non-polar                solvent, drying, and evaporating solvents to                provide crude                (R)-7-(2-bromoethyl)-1,4-dioxaspiro[4.5]decane of                formula (III), (b) reacting the compound (III)                dissolved in a reaction solvent with aqueous                hydrochloric acid at ambient temperature, removing                the reaction solvent in vacuo, extracting the                aqueous phase with an organic solvent, washing the                organic phase with saturated aqueous sodium                bicarbonate, and brine, and evaporating solvents to                provide crude (R)-3-(2-bromoethyl)cyclohexan-1-one                of formula (IV), (c) reacting the compound (IV)                dissolved in the solvent (s1) with a mixture of                triphenylphosphine, bis(pinacolato)diboron, copper                iodide, and lithium methanolate suspended in the                solvent (s2), at elevated temperature, filtering,                adding the solvent (s3) and aqueous hydrochloric                acid, performing phase separation, extracting the                aqueous phase with the solvent (s4), washing the                extract with brine, evaporating solvents,                precipitating triphenylphosphine using a non-polar                solvent, drying, evaporating volatiles, and                distilling under reduced pressure, to provide                (R)-3-(2-(4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1,3,2-dioxaborolan-2-yl)ethyl)-cyclohexan-1-one                of formula (V), (d) reacting the compound (V) with                dimethyl carbonate, in the presence of a strong                base in a solvent at elevated temperature,                quenching the reaction with saturated aqueous                ammonium chloride, and extraction with a solvent,                to provide methyl                (4R)-2-oxo-4-(2-(4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1,3,2-dioxaborolan-2-yl)ethyl)cyclohexane-1-carboxylate                of formula (VI), (e) dissolving the compound (VI)                in a solvent, adding ammonium acetate, cooling the                solution, and treating with tert-butyl isocyanide                to afford the crude compound of the compound (I),                and (f) purifying the crude compound of the                compound (I) by crystallization from an ether                solvent. INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are included for the                following:
   preparation of the solid crystalline                (1A,2S,5R)-1-amino-5-[2-(dihydroxyboranyl)ethyl]-2-[(dimethylamino)methyl]cyclohexane-1-carboxylic                acid of formula (VII) in anhydrous form A, which                involves performing the steps (a-f), (g) reacting                the compound (I) with diisobutylaluminum hydride in                dichloromethane at a reduced temperature, and                quenching the reaction with an organic acid, (h)                treating the reaction mixture with dimethylamine at                a reduced temperature, (i) treating the reaction                mixture with a reducing agent, and quenching the                reaction with saturated aqueous sodium carbonate,                (j) extracting the reaction mixture with a solvent,                to afford crude                (1R,2S,5R)-1-acetamido-N-(tert-butyl)-2-[(dimethylamino)methyl]-5-(2-(4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1,3,2-dioxaborolan-2-yl)ethyl)cyclohexane-1-carboxamide                of formula (VIII), (k) dissolving the crude                compound of the compound (VIII) in an ether                solvent, and extracting the mixture into aqueous                hydrochloric acid, (l) refluxing the extract in                aqueous 6 M hydrochloric acid, cooling, and                evaporating in vacuo, (m) purifying the residue on                an ion-exchange resin column, eluting with aqueous                ammonia, and evaporating in vacuo, to yield the                crude product of the compound (VII), (n)                crystallizing the crude product of the compound                (VII) from water/acetone system, (o)                recrystallizing the partially purified product of                the compound (VII) from (water+methanol)/acetone                system, and (p) drying the crystals to yield the                solid crystalline compound of the compound (VII) in                anhydrous form A; and
   the solid crystalline (VII).
                                                                                                                     
                                                                                                                     
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: In step (a), the                  organic base is a nitrogen heterocycle,                  preferably pyridine, the reaction solvent is                  dichloromethane, the quenching reagent is an                  alcohol, preferably methanol, and the non-polar                  solvent is an alkane, preferably n-hexane. In                  step (b), the reaction solvent is acetonitrile,                  the organic solvent is an ether, preferably                  methyl tert-butyl ether, and the mild base is                  sodium bicarbonate. In the step (c), the solvents                  (s1, s2) are dimethylformamide, the solvents (s3,                  s4) are an ether, preferably methyl tert-butyl                  ether, and the non-polar solvent is an alkane,                  preferably n-hexane. In the step (d), the strong                  base is sodium hydride, the reaction solvent is                  dimethylformamide, and the extraction solvent is                  methyl tert-butyl ether. In the step (e), the                  reaction solvent is 2,2,2-trifluoroethanol. In                  the step (f), the ether solvent is diisopropyl                  ether. In the step (g), the organic acid is                  acetic acid. In the step (i), the reducing agent                  is sodium triacetoxyborohydride. In the step (j),                  the extracting solvent is dichloromethane. In the                  step (k), the ether solvent is methyl tert-butyl                  ether. In the step (m), the ion-exchange resin is                  DOWEX 50WX8(RTM: strongly acidic cation                  exchanger) regenerated with 1 M hydrochloric                  acid, and the aqueous ammonia is 1 M in ammonia.                  Preferred Process: The order of steps (g), (h),                  (i) is replaced by dissolving sodium                  triacetoxyborohydride in dichloromethane and                  cooling to -75&#176; C, adding under flow                  conditions a stream of combined compound (I),                  dimethylamine in tetrahydrofuran, acetic acid,                  and diisobutylaluminum hydride in                  dichloromethane, cooling to 0&#176; C, and                  warming up the reaction mixture to the ambient                  temperature. Preferred Conditions: In the step                  (c), the elevated temperature is 40&#176; C.                  In the step (d), the reaction temperature is                  75&#176; C or less. In the step (e), the                  reaction temperature is 0&#176; C. In the                  steps (g, h), the reaction temperature is                  -75&#176; C. Preferred Properties: The solid                  crystalline has (i) X-ray powder diffraction                  peaks at 7.39, 8.55, 9.49, 12.83, 14.71, 16.86,                  17.37, 17.88, 19.11, 19.88, 20.55, 21.19, 22.18,                  22.87, 23.66, 24.52, 25.73, 26.09, 26.94, 28.19,                  28.61, 28.88, 29.69, 30.28, 31.45, 32.02, 33.06,                  33.36, 34.05, 34.52, 35.15, 36.20, 37.52, 38.95,                  40.54, 41.76, (ii) infrared bands at 3134, 2992,                  2918, 2859, 2826, 2789, 2764, 2727, 1584, 1522,                  1458, 1404, 1369, 1344, 1310, 1263, 1202, 1159,                  1105, 1074, 1028, 993, 887, 845, 762, 731 cm-1,                  and (iii) differential scanning calorimetry trace                  with two broad endothermic events of onset at                  141.9&#176; C and peak at 173.7&#176; C,                  and onset at 237.4&#176; C and peak at                  254.1&#176; C.
   ACTIVITY - Cardiovascular-Gen.; Vulnerary;                Gastrointestinal-Gen.; Immunosuppressive;                Immunomodulator; Anti-HIV; Antiinflammatory;                Hepatotropic; Virucide; Antibacterial;                Respiratory-Gen.; Cytostatic.
   MECHANISM OF ACTION - Arginase 1 (ARG1) inhibitor; ARG2 inhibitor.                No biological data given.
   EXAMPLE - A suitable reactor was charged with 478 g                crude methyl                (4R)-2-oxo-4-(2-(4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1,3,2-dioxaborolan-2-yl)ethyl)cyclohexane-1-carboxylate                in 3770 mL 2,2,2-trifluoroethanol under an argon                atmosphere. Then, 368 g ammonium acetate was added                at ambient temperature. The obtained mixture was                then stirred at ambient temperature for at least 30                minutes, until all of ammonium acetate was                dissolved. Then, the reaction mixture was cooled to                0&#176; C and 216 ml tert-Butyl isocyanide was                added in one portion. The mixture was stirred at                0&#176; C for 48 hours. Then, the reaction                mixture was concentrated to 1/5 of initial volume                in vacuo and 1 L n-hexane was added. The obtained                mixture was stirred for 10 minutes and concentrated                in vacuo. The obtained mixture was loaded onto                silica gel column for filtration. The compound (I)                was eluted, the fractions containing product were                combined and evaporated to give 700 g crude product                of the compound (I). The crude product of the                compound (I) was dissolved in 1200 mL diisopropyl                ether under an argon atmosphere. The resulting                mixture was heated to 50&#176; C and stirred                for 60 minutes. After this time the mixture was                slowly cooled to 0&#176; C and stirred at this                temperature overnight. Then, the suspension was                cooled to -20&#176; C and stirred for 3 hours.                The suspension was then filtrated and the obtained                residue was washed with cold 400 mL diisopropyl                ether to obtain 231 g (32% yield) compound (I) as                white crystals.

TI Molecularly-imprinted crosslinked micelle used in            material for hydrolyzing cellulose, comprises imprint            of functional or structural analogue of glycan, and            binding unit and acid unit, where acid unit is proximal            to glycosidic bond of glycan during binding of glycan            to binding unit.
AU ZHAO Y
AE UNIV IOWA STATE RES FOUND INC (IOWA-C)
AB    NOVELTY - A molecularly-imprinted crosslinked micelle                selective for glycan, comprises an imprint of a                functional or structural analogue of the glycan,                and a binding unit and an acid unit, where the                binding unit is bindable to the glycan, and the                acid unit is proximal to a glycosidic bond of the                glycan during binding of the glycan to the binding                unit.

   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Alkylation of nitrogen-containing heterocyclic                compounds in a visible light-mediated microreaction                device, comprises (i) dissolving                nitrogen-containing heterocyclic compound I i.e.                quinoline or isoquinoline compound of formula (I)                and additive II i.e.                2,3,7-trifluoro-5-phenyl-5H-thianthren-5-ium                tetrafluoroborate compound of formula (II) in                organic solvent III i.e. compound of formula (III)                to obtain homogeneous solution A, (ii) dissolving                sodium iodide and triphenylphosphine in organic                solvent III to obtain homogeneous solution B, (iii)                pumping the homogeneous solution A and homogeneous                solution B into micro-reaction device, mixing in                the micro-mixer of the micro-reaction device,                passing into the micro-channel reactor of the                micro-reaction device under the irradiation of                visible light for reaction, and (iv) collecting the                effluent of the microchannel reactor described in                step (iii) to obtain N-containing heterocyclic                alkylation product i.e. substituted quinoline                compound of formula (IV) with the introduction of                the corresponding solvent group.
   R1, R2and R = H, 1-6C alkyl (optionally                mono-substituted, di-substituted or tri-substituted                with cyano or halo), ester, cyano, halo, biphenyl                or phenyl (optionally mono-substituted,                di-substituted or tri-substituted with 1-6C alkyl,                halo or cyano group).

The purified protein was bound and                washed in the column with binding buffer A (20 mM                phosphate buffer, 20 mM imidazole, 500 mM NaCl, 1                mM PMSF and beta-mercaptoethanol, pH of 7.4) and                eluted in a gradient of elution buffer B (20 mM                phosphate buffer, 500 mM imidazole, 500 mM NaCl, 1                mM PMSF and beta-mercaptoethanol, pH of 7.4). Then                using a PD-10 column, the buffer was changed to a                50 mM Tris-HCl, pH of 7.4. Candidates expression                and purification were analyzed on 12%                polyacrylamide gel by electrophoresis.

TI Preparation of maleic acid or its derivative            involves performing electrochemical oxidation in            electrolyte solution of furanic compound into            intermediates, and performing chemo-catalytic            oxidation.
AU URBANUS J
   CROCKATT M
   GOETHEER E L V
   LATSUZBAIA R
AE NEDERLANDSE ORG TOEGEPAST NATUURWETENSCH (NEDE-C)

   NEDERLANDSE ORG TOEGEPAST NATUURWETENSCH (NEDE-C)
AB    NOVELTY - Preparation of maleic acid or its derivative                involves (1) performing an electrochemical                oxidation in an electrolyte solution of a furanic                compound (II) into one or more intermediates, and                (2) performing a chemo-catalytic oxidation of the                intermediates.
   USE - Preparation of maleic acid.
   ADVANTAGE - The method enables simple, economical and                effective preparation of maleic acid or its                derivative with high yield even at mild                condition.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Preparation of maleic acid of formula (I) or                its derivative involves (1) performing an                electrochemical oxidation in an electrolyte                solution of a furanic compound of formula (II) into                one or more intermediates, and (2) performing a                chemo-catalytic oxidation of the                intermediates.
   R =H, CH 2 OH, CO 2 H or CHO;and
   R =H, OH, 1-6c alkyl or O(1-6C alkyl), or                ester, ether, amido, acid halide, anhydrido,                carboximidato, nitrile, or their salts.
                                                                                                                     
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - INORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The electrolyte                  solution is free of mediator. The mediator                  comprises one or more of vanadate, vanadium oxide                  (e.g. V2O5, VO2), molybdate, chromate (CrO),                  dichromate (CrO42), permanganates (MnO4+)                  manganate (MnO4+), manganese salts (Mn2+),                  tungstate (WO42-), iodates (IO3-), chlorate                  (ClO-), chloride-chlorine couple (Cl-/Cl2),                  bromate (BrO-), bromide-bromine couple (Br/Br2),                  peroxy disulfate (S2O82-) ozone (O3), cobalt                  salts (Co2+/Co3+), cerium salts (Ce3+/Ce4+),                  preferably vanadium oxide, sodium molybdate,                  and/or potassium dichromate. The mediator is                  immobilized on the surface of the electrode. The                  electrolyte solution is an aqueous electrolyte                  solution. The derivative of maleic acid is one or                  more chosen from maleic anhydride, fumaric acid,                  succinic acid, succinonitrile, putrescine, malic                  acid, and salts, anhydrides, amide, imides or                  esters of any of these compounds. The electrolyte                  solution comprises an acid, preferably a mineral                  acid, a mineral acid chosen from hydrochloric                  acid (HCl), nitric acid (HNO3), phosphoric acid                  (H3PO4), sulfuric acid (H2SO4), boric acid                  (H3BO3), hydrofluoric acid (HF), hydrobromic acid                  (HBr), perchloric acid (HClO4), and hydroiodic                  acid (HI). Preferred Process: The chemo-catalytic                  oxidation is performed by using oxygen as an                  oxidation agent in the presence of a catalyst                  preferably comprising a transition metal, metal                  salt, metal oxide or a phosphate, metal is chosen                  from cobalt, manganese, vanadium, molybdenum,                  copper, silver, gold, palladium, platinum                  ruthenium, and gold. The method further involves                  performing extraction of the intermediates with                  an organic solvent and preferably carrying out                  the step (2) in the organic solvent, performing                  one-step electrochemical oxidation of furanic                  compound, and isolating the maleic acid or the                  derivative to provide the isolated maleic acid or                  derivative and using electrolyte solution,                  preferably recycling the used electrolyte                  solution, optionally after an intermediate                  purification, and chemo-catalytic oxidation of                  5-hydroxy-2(5H)-furanone and/or cis-&#946;                  -formylacrylic acid to maleic acid or a                  derivative. The step (1) is directly providing                  maleic anhydride of formula (V). The step (1) is                  performed by photoelectrical oxidation. The                  electrochemical oxidation is carried out at a pH                  of less than 4, preferably 2 or less, initial                  concentration of the furanic compound in the                  electrolyte solution is 0.01-5 mol/L, preferably                  0.3-2 mol/L. The chemo-catalytic oxidation is                  carried out at a temperature of 20-150&#176;                  C, preferably 50-100&#176; C. The                  electrochemical oxidation is carried out with one                  or more working electrodes comprising lead oxide,                  for instance lead oxide, and/or boron doped                  diamond (BDD). The process is a continuous                  process which is carried out in a continuous                  reactor system, in which the isolation of the                  maleic acid is carried out continuously in situ                  and/or in parallel, at the start of the process,                  the electrolyte solution comprising only water,                  the furanic compound, the acid and optionally an                  inorganic salt.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            
   TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The intermediates                  comprise 5-hydroxy-2(5H)-furanone of formula                  (III) and/or cis-6-formylacrylic acid of formula                  (IV).

TI Synthesizing 2,5-furandicarboxylic acid comprises            e.g. mixing furfural, hydrogenation catalyst and            solvent, replacing air in the reactor with inert gas,            heating reactor, reacting, and obtaining methyl furan            by separation.
AU ZHAO C
   LI B
   ZHAO L
AE UNIV EAST CHINA NORMAL (UYEN-C)
AB    NOVELTY - Synthesizing 2,5-furandicarboxylic acid                comprises (i) mixing furfural, hydrogenation                catalyst and solvent into reactor, replacing air in                the reactor with inert gas, replacing inert gas                with hydrogen, heating reactor, reacting, and                obtaining methyl furan by separation, (ii) mixing                methylfuran with acetic anhydride, dissolving in a                solvent, passing into an atmospheric pressure fixed                bed reactor equipped with a solid acid catalyst,                and obtaining 5-methyl- 2-acetylfuran, (iii) adding                5-methyl-2-acetylfuran, supported metal catalyst                and solvent into reactor, and passing inert gas to                replace the air in the reactor, introducing                hydrogen gas to replace the inert gas, maintaining                hydrogen pressure at 1-4 MPa, heating reactor and                reacting, separating, and obtaining                2-methyl-5-ethylfuran, and (iv) adding                2-methyl-5-ethylfuran intov reactor equipped with                Teflon lining, reacting, and separating.
   USE - The method is useful for synthesizing                2,5-furandicarboxylic acid.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Synthesizing 2,5-furandicarboxylic acid                comprises (i) mixing furfural, hydrogenation                catalyst and solvent into reactor, replacing air in                the reactor with inert gas, replacing inert gas                with hydrogen, maintaining hydrogen pressure at                0.1-4MPa, heating reactor to 180-280 degrees C,                reacting for 0.1-24 hours, and obtaining methyl                furan by separation, (ii) mixing methylfuran with                acetic anhydride, dissolving in a solvent, passing                into an atmospheric pressure fixed bed reactor                equipped with a solid acid catalyst, where the                reactor temperature is 30-150 degrees C, and                obtaining 5-methyl- 2-acetylfuran, (iii) adding                5-methyl-2-acetylfuran, supported metal catalyst                and solvent into reactor, and passing inert gas to                replace the air in the reactor, introducing                hydrogen gas to replace the inert gas, maintaining                hydrogen pressure at 1-4 MPa, heating reactor to                180-280 degrees C and reacting for 5 minutes to 24                hours, separating, and obtaining                2-methyl-5-ethylfuran, and (iv) adding                2-methyl-5-ethylfuran intov reactor equipped with                Teflon lining, reacting under the catalytic action                of oxidant for 0.1-16 hours, and separating.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - INORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: In the step (i), the                  hydrogenation catalyst is a supported catalyst                  containing at least one metals including copper,                  nickel, iron, cobalt and ruthenium, the total                  metal loading is 0.05-50% of the carrier mass,                  the carrier used is activated carbon, nano-carbon                  fiber, silica, titania, alumina and/or pure                  silicon molecular sieve. In the step (i), the                  mass ratio of hydrogenation catalyst to furfural                  is 5:1-1:20. In the step (i), and (ii), the                  solvent is methanol, ethanol, tetrahydrofuran,                  n-hexane and dodecane. In the step (ii), the                  solid acid catalyst is a solid molecular sieve,                  phosphotungstic acid, phosphomolybdic acid or                  supported catalyst, and the supported catalyst                  uses phosphotungstic acid or phosphomolybdic acid                  as the active center. The solid molecular sieve                  is ZSM-5, HBEA, HY, HSUY, and/or HMOR. In the                  step (ii), the solvent is chloroalkane, and the                  chloroalkane is ethyl chloride, ethylene                  dichloride, chloroform, chloropropane or                  dichloropropane. In the step (iii), the active                  center of the supported metal catalyst is copper,                  nickel, iron, cobalt, and/or ruthenium, the total                  metal loading accounted for the carrier mass                  ratio is 0.05-50%, and the carrier used is                  activated carbon, nano-carbon fiber, silica,                  titania, alumina, and/or pure silicon molecular                  sieve. In the step (iv), the oxidant is potassium                  permanganate acid solution or hydrogen peroxide                  solution. In the step (iv), the oxidant is 0.1-4                  Mpa oxygen, metal salt of cobalt, a metal salt                  containing manganese, a metal salt containing                  bromine or a solution of hydrogen bromide is                  added into reactor, where mole percentage of                  cobalt, manganese or bromine in the raw material                  2-methyl-5-ethylfuran is 0.1-10%, solvent is also                  added into reactor, and the solvent is                  acetonitrile, 1,2-dichloroethane, chloroform,                  acetic acid, propionic acid, or n-butyric                  acid.

   KRAUS G A
AE UNIV IOWA STATE RES FOUND INC (IOWA-C)
AB    NOVELTY - Preparing (P1) substituted dicarboxylic acid                compounds (I), comprises: providing either                substituted metal carboxylates (II) or substituted                metal dicarboxylates (III); providing an oxide of                carbon; providing a copper(I) containing compound;                and reacting (II) or (III) with the oxide of carbon                in the presence of the copper(I) containing                compound to form (I).
   USE - The process is useful for preparing (I).
   ADVANTAGE - The process: provides sustainable,                cadmium-free, highly selective catalytic conditions                for disproportionation or isomerization of aromatic                carboxylates; and provides reactor configuration                that facilitates in situ solid phase mixing in                combination with thermal and pressurized conditions                that lead to additional improvements in yield and                selectivity with lower quantities of                catalyst.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Preparing (P1) substituted dicarboxylic acid                compounds of formula (I) or their salts, comprises:                providing either substituted metal carboxylates of                formula (II) or substituted metal dicarboxylates of                formula (III); providing an oxide of carbon;                providing a copper(I) containing compound; and                reacting (II) or (III) with the oxide of carbon in                the presence of the copper(I) containing compound                to form (I).
   Ring A = aryl or heteroaryl (both optionally                substituted by 1-3 times having 1-6C alkyl, 3-8C                cycloalkyl, aryl or heteroaryl; and
   M = metal.
   INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are also included                for:
   (1) preparing (P2) substituted ester compounds                of formula (IV), comprising providing (I),                providing an alkylating agent, reacting (I) with                the alkylating agent to form (IV); and
   (2) preparing (IV), comprising providing (II),                providing an oxide of carbon, providing a copper(I)                containing compound, providing an alkylating agent,                and reacting (II) with the oxide of carbon in the                presence of the copper(I) containing compound and                the alkylating agent to form (IV).
   R = 1-6C alkyl; and
   W1 = OH, halo, -OSO2CF3 or OSO2C6H4Me.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - INORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The oxide of carbon is                  carbon dioxide. The copper(I) containing compound                  is a copper(I) halide, preferably copper iodide.                  The copper(I) containing compound comprises                  0.001-25 mol.%, preferably 5-15 mol.%.
   TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Process: The process (P1) further                  comprises: providing an alkylating agent,                  reacting (I) with the alkylating agent to form                  substituted ester compounds of formula (IV),                  where the alkylating agent is substituted alkyl                  compounds of formula (R-W1) (IA) or an alkene in                  the presence of p-toluenesulfonic acid; filtering                  the dissolved reaction mixture; acidifying the                  filtered reaction mixture using a mineral acid;                  and mixing (II) with the copper(I) containing                  compound prior to the reacting to form a reaction                  mixture. In the process (P1): the reacting leads                  to formation of a reaction mixture, where the                  process (P1) further comprises dissolving the                  reaction mixture in the hot water; the reacting                  is carried out at a pressure of 1-60 bar,                  preferably 30-50 bar; the reacting is carried out                  at a temperature above 250 degrees C and below                  the decomposition temperature of (II), preferably                  above 290 degrees C to below 360 degrees C; the                  reacting is carried out for 0.0005-24 hours,                  preferably 0.05-18 hours; and the mixing is                  conducted for at least 5 minutes. In the process                  (P2), the providing (I) comprises providing (II),                  providing an oxide of carbon, providing a                  copper(I) containing compound, and reacting (II)                  with the oxide of carbon in the presence of the                  copper(I) containing compound to form (I).
   Preferred Definitions:
   M = K or Na.
   EXAMPLE - 262 mg Dried potassium furoate and 33 mg                copper (I) iodide were mixed in mortar and pestle,                and then transferred to a test tube. Test tube was                placed in an aluminum block inside a Parr reactor.                Reactor was charged and vented with carbon dioxide                three times to remove the ambient atmosphere.                Reactor was pressurized with carbon dioxide (40                bar) and then heated at the desired temperature for                a set time (e.g. 300 degrees C for 16 hours).                Vessel was allowed to cool to room temperature, and                then the pressure was released. Crude product was                typically a reddish-black solid, which was                dissolved in hot water. Solution was filtered                through charcoal and a glass frit and then                acidified with hydrochloric acid (aqueous) to                precipitate a white solid. Suspension was allowed                to stand for 1 hour, the mixture was centrifuged,                the supernatant was decanted, and the solid washed                with water. White solid was dried to obtain                2,5-furan dicarboxylic acid with a yield of 73.5                mg.

TI New peptide construct comprises bicyclic structure            resulting from attaching linker having 1st end and 2nd            end comprising two reactive groups to polypeptide, for            making phage display complex, and for measuring yield            of reaction.
AU DERDA R
   MUKHERJEE R
   TRIANA V
AE UNIV ALBERTA (UYAL-C)
   UNIV ALBERTA (UYAL-C)
   UNIV ALBERTA (UYAL-C)
   UNIV ALBERTA (UYAL-C)
   48HOUR DISCOVERY INC (FOUR-Non-standard)
AB    NOVELTY - A peptide construct comprising a bicyclic                structure resulting from attaching a linker having                a 1st end and a 2nd end comprising two reactive                groups to a polypeptide, is new. The 1st end is                ligated to a terminus of the polypeptide by a                covalent bond, and both reactive groups of the                linker 2nd end are each reacted to side chain                residues of the polypeptide.
   USE - The peptide construct is useful for making a                phage display complex; for measuring the yield of a                reaction after at least one step; and for                identifying a complex which is capable of binding                to a ligand (all claimed).
   ADVANTAGE - Low pH at a reduction step maximizes the yield                of the bicyclization reaction. Increase in pH after                the reduction cleanly affords the bicyclic products                and no visible unreacted starting material or                byproducts by liquid chromatography-mass                spectrometry (LCMS).
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are included for:
   making a phage display complex, which                involves: (i) providing a phage particle comprising                a polypeptide having a terminus; (ii) providing a                linker having a 1st end and a 2nd end comprising                two reactive groups, and forming an intermediate                complex by ligating the linker 1st end with the                polypeptide terminus to form a covalent bond, and                (iii) forming a bicyclic structure from the                intermediate complex by reacting both reactive                groups of the linker 2nd end to side chain residues                of the polypeptide;
   measuring the yield of a reaction after at                least one step which involves exposing the                polypeptide to a capture reagent which is reactive                to unreacted polypeptide but not reacted                polypeptide and measuring the incorporation of                capture agent with an affinity reagent;
   a genetically encoded library of ligands                comprising different peptide sequences forming a                bicyclic structure;
   identifying a complex which is capable of                binding to a ligand, which involves: (i) contacting                a library of complexes w ith the ligand, and (ii)                selecting those complexes which bind to the ligand                (iii) identifying the structure of a binding                complex by sequencing a nucleic acid that encodes                the structure of peptide and/or linker; and
   a nodal antagonist peptide comprising the                complex formed from polypeptide comprising                Ser-Pro-Cys-Gln-Arg-Gly-His-Met-Phe-Cys or                Ser-Tyr-Cys-Lys-Arg-Ala-His-Lys-Asn-Cys (SEQ ID                NOS: not defined) and a linker comprising                TSL6.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - BIOTECHNOLOGY - Preferred Components: The two 2nd end                  reactive groups are identical. The polypeptide                  terminus is N-terminus and the side chain                  residues are cysteine, lysine or tyrosine. The                  polypeptide comprises two cysteine residues and                  an N-terminal serine or threonine residue, where                  the N-terminal serine or threonine is 1st                  converted to an aldehyde by selective oxidation                  and where the 2nd end reactive groups are                  covalently bonded to the cysteine residues. The                  construct is attached to a carrier such as a                  phage particle bearing the polypeptide externally                  and including a nucleic acid encoding the                  polypeptide. It is a RNA display compound, the                  RNA display bearing the polypeptide; and                  including an RNA sequence encoding the                  polypeptide, and linked to the polypeptide. It is                  a DNA display compound the DNA display bearing                  the polypeptide; and including a DNA encoding the                  polypeptide, linked to the polypeptide. The                  polypeptide terminus is a C-terminus and the side                  chain residues are cysteine or lysine, and the                  linker 1st end comprises a reactive group with                  reactivity to C-terminus in acidic pH and the                  linker 2nd end comprises two electrophilic groups                  that react specifically with thiol or amine                  residues. The polypeptide comprises a terminal                  serine residue and two cysteine residues                  separated by 2-5 amino acid residues.It comprises                  only natural proteogenic amino acids and/or which                  does not comprise free N-terminus. The capture                  agent comprises a reactive group and affinity                  handle paired with the affinity reagent such as                  biotin-streptavidin, FLAG peptide-anti-FLAG                  antibody sulfonamide-carbonic anhydrase,                  methotrexate-dehydrofolate reductase (DHFR),                  preferably where the affinity handle or the                  affinity reagent is immobilized on a solid                  support, such as agarose beads. Preferably,                  &#8805; 2 peptides P1 and P2 are separately                  encoded by DNA sequences. The library comprises                  &#8805; 2 different peptide sequences P1 and                  P2, are each separately modified with &#8805;                  2 different linkers L1 and L2, forming                  &#8805; 4 distinct constructs P1L1, P1L2,                  P1L2, and P2L2. Linker L1 is reacted with two                  peptides that are associated with pre-defined                  nucleic acid code B1 and linker L2 is reacted                  with two peptides that are associated with                  pre-defined nucleic acid code B2. The nucleic                  acid codes are silent genetic barcodes. Preferred                  Method: Steps (ii) and (iii) are independent and                  sequential. Steps (ii) and (iii) are performed at                  different pHs. The terminus of the polypeptide is                  an N-terminus and is oxidized to aldehyde before                  the ligation step, and the ligation step involves                  mixing the polypeptide comprising N-temiinal                  aldehyde with the linker in aqueous buffer of                  acidic pH. The intermediate complex is purified,                  such as by size-exclusion purification, such as                  by gel filtration or dialysis in aqueous buffer                  of acidic pH, such as below about pH of 5. The                  purified intermediate complex is reduced with a                  reducing agent such as                  tris(2-carboxyethyl)phosphine (TCEP) (reduction                  step), prior to the bicyclization step. The                  reduced intermediate complex is exposed to pH of                  7 or above to induce bicyclization, where the 2nd                  end reactive groups each comprise a                  thiol-reactive group and the polypeptide                  comprises two cysteine residues. At least one                  step is the oxidation step involving: (i)                  exposure of phage to aminooxy biotin (AOB) after                  the oxidation step (ii) diluting the reaction by                  at least one order of magnitude, (iii) adding                  streptavidin beads, and (iv) measuring the number                  of phage particles remaining after capture, where                  the difference in number of phage retained on the                  streptavidin beads constitutes the yield of                  oxidation step (the fraction of phage particles                  that acquired an aldehyde group). At least one                  step is the ligation step involving: (i) exposure                  of phage to AOB before and after the ligation                  step, (ii) diluting the reactions by at least one                  order of magnitude, (iii) adding streptavidin                  beads, and (iv) measuring the number of phage                  particles remaining after capture, where the                  difference m number of phage retained on the                  streptavidin beads constitutes the yield of                  ligation step (the fraction of phage particles                  that lost an aldehyde group). At least one step                  is the reduction step which involves: (i)                  exposure of phage to biotin-iodoacetamide (BIA),                  before and after the reduction step: (ii)                  diluting the reactions by at least one order of                  magnitude, (iii) adding streptavidin beads, and                  (iv) measuring the number of phage particles                  remaining after capture, where the difference in                  number of phage retained on the streptavidin                  beads constitutes the yield of reduction step                  (the fraction of phage particles that acquired                  thiol groups during the reduction step). At least                  one step is the bicyclization step involvin: (i)                  exposure of phage to BIA or biotin-thiol (BSH),                  before and after bicyclization step, (ii)                  diluting the reactions by at least one order of                  magnitude, (iii) adding streptavidin beads (iv)                  measuring the number of phage particles remaining                  after capture, where the number of phage retained                  on the streptavidin beads constitutes the yield                  of bicyclization step (the fraction of phage                  particles that lost thiol groups during the                  bicyclization step).
   TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The linker reactive                  groups are electrophilic groups reactive to                  thiol, amine or phenol. The linker 1st end                  comprises an aldehyde reactive group such as                  oxime, hydrazine, 2-amino benzamidoxime,                  phosphonium ylide, sulfur ylide, nitrogen ylides                  or any other carbon nucleophile and carbenoid                  reagents known to be reactive with aldehydes and                  stable in aqueous environment. The reactive group                  with reactivity to C-terminus in acidic pH is a                  C-terminal-selective photoredoxdecarboxylative                  conjugate addition. The linker electrophilic                  groups are reactive with thiol in water, such as                  halokctone, haloacetamide, halobenzyl, a Michael                  acceptor comprising a conjugated C-C double bond                  including maleimides, actylates, carbonylacrylic                  reagents, 3-arylpropiolonitriles, allenamides                  fluoroarene, chlorotetrazines,                  Julia-Kocienski-like reagents, 2-azidoacrylates,                  organometallic palladium reagents organo gold(I)                  reagents, conversion of thiols to dehydroalanines                  (Dha) followed by conjugate addition to Dha. The                  linker electrophilic groups are reactive with                  amine in water, such as N-bydroxysuccinimide                  esters, and esters, perfluoroaryl esters,                  perfluoroarenes, ketenes, ortho-phthalaldehydes,                  or strain-release amine modifying agents. The                  linker electrophilic groups are reactive with                  phenol in water, such as allylpaladium,                  diazodicarboxylate, diazonium salts,                  aniline-formaldehyde hemiaminal, rhodium                  carbenoids, dirhodium metallopeptide catalysts,                  manganese-catalyzed C-H alkynylation, Waser's                  reagent,                  1-[(triisopropylsilyl)ethynyl]-1,2-benziodoxol-3(1H)-one                  (TIPS-EBX), under gold(I) catalysis, selective                  ruthenium-(II)-catalyzed C-H activation,                  palladium(II) acetate catalyzed C-H activation                  with aryl iodides in water. The linker comprises                  an alkyl chain having 1-6 carbon atoms. Capture                  agent reactive group is an aldehyde reactive                  group such as aminooxy group or a thiol reactive                  group such as iodoacetamide.
   EXAMPLE - No suitable example given.

PN CN110713447-A; CN110713447-B
TI Preparing p-methylthiobenzaldehyde comprises e.g.            adding phase transfer catalyst quaternary ammonium salt            or quaternary phosphine salt into sodium thiosulfate            solution, subjecting to acid hydrolysis and collecting            lower organic phase.
AU ZHANG X
   LIU C
   ZHAO X
   WANG Z
AE SHANDONG GUOBANG PHARM CO LTD (SHAN-Non-standard)
   GUOBANG MEDICINE & CHEM IND GROUP CO LTD (GUOB-Non-standard)
   GUOBANG PHARMA LTD (GUOB-Non-standard)
AB    NOVELTY - Preparing p-methylthiobenzaldehyde comprises                (i) adding phase transfer catalyst quaternary                ammonium salt or quaternary phosphine salt into                10-30 wt.% sodium thiosulfate aqueous solution,                adding p-chlorobenzaldehyde, maintaining the                reaction at 50-90&#176; C for 2-6 hours to                obtain bunte salt, where the molar ratio of sodium                thiosulfate and p-chlorobenzaldehyde is 1-1.3; (ii)                subjecting bunte salt to acid hydrolysis at                50-100&#176; C for 1-3 hours, adding reduced                metal zinc powder or iron into p-chlorobenzaldehyde                in a molar ratio of 0.001-0.10, cooling the                powderat 40-70&#176; C for 1-3 hours,                controlling the pH value at 0-2 in the process of                acid hydrolysis and reduction, adding alkali to                neutralize for 0.5-1.5 hours, where the pH value is                controlled to 7-10; (iii) performing methylation                reaction with the methylation reagent at                80-120&#176; C, cooling to 30-40&#176; C,                allowing the layers to stand, collecting lower                organic phase and adding water-absorbing agent to                absorb water.
   USE - The method is useful for preparing                p-methylthiobenzaldehyde.
   ADVANTAGE - The method avoids using sodium methyl                mercaptan as a substitution agent and smell of                sodium methyl mercaptan; has easily available raw                material; is simple; and improves high. The                p-methylthiobenzaldehyde is clean and                environmentally-friendly.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Preparing p-methylthiobenzaldehyde comprises                (i) adding phase transfer catalyst quaternary                ammonium salt or quaternary phosphine salt into                10-30 wt.% sodium thiosulfate aqueous solution,                adding p-chlorobenzaldehyde, maintaining the                reaction at 50-90&#176; C for 2-6 hours to                obtain bunte salt, where the molar ratio of sodium                thiosulfate and p-chlorobenzaldehyde is 1-1.3; (ii)                subjecting bunte salt to acid hydrolysis at                50-100&#176; C for 1-3 hours, adding reduced                metal zinc powder or iron into p-chlorobenzaldehyde                in a molar ratio of 0.001-0.10, cooling the                powderat 40-70&#176; C for 1-3 hours,                controlling the pH value at 0-2 in the process of                acid hydrolysis and reduction, adding alkali to                neutralize for 0.5-1.5 hours to obtain sodium                p-formylphenyl mercaptan, where the pH value is                controlled to 7-10 during the neutralization                reaction; (iii) performing methylation reaction                with the methylation reagent at 80-120&#176; C                under reaction pressure of 0.2-1.0 MPa for 6-10                hours, cooling to 30-40&#176; C, allowing the                layers to stand, collecting lower organic phase and                adding water-absorbing agent to absorb water.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - INORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: In the step (i), the                  molar ratio of sodium thiosulfate and                  p-chlorobenzaldehyde is 1.1-1.2, the molar ratio                  of the phase transfer catalyst and                  p-chlorobenzaldehyde is 0.001-0.10 and the phase                  transfer catalyst is tetrabutylammonium chloride,                  tetrabutylammonium bromide, tetrabutylphosphine                  bromide or tetrabutylphosphine chloride. In the                  step (ii), the base is 32 wt.% sodium hydroxide                  aqueous solution and the pH value is controlled                  to be 8-9 during the neutralization reaction. In                  the step (iii), the water absorbing agent is                  sodium sulfate. Preferred Method: In the step                  (i), the reaction is carried out at                  60-80&#176; C for 4 hours and the                  concentration of the sodium thiosulfate aqueous                  solution is 10-20 wt.%. In the step (ii), the                  bunte salt is subjected to acid hydrolysis at                  85-95&#176; C for 2 hours and the acid used                  is hydrochloric acid or sulfuric acid. In the                  step (ii), the reducing metal is iron powder, the                  molar ratio of the reduction metal and                  p-chlorobenzaldehyde is 0.005-0.015,, the                  reduction is carried out at 55-65&#176; C for                  2 hours and the pH value during acid hydrolysis                  and reduction is 0.0-0.5. In the step (iii), the                  methylation reagent is methyl chloride and the                  reaction is carried out at 90-100&#176; C                  under reaction pressure 0.3-0.6 MPa for 8                  hours.

AB    NOVELTY - Allyl alcohol amine formate-alkenylated                product (A) is new.
   USE - (A) is useful in preparing natural                products.
   ADVANTAGE - (A) is suitable for efficient chemical                modification of relatively complex structures with                biologically active compounds.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Allyl alcohol amine formate-alkenylated                product (A) of formulae (I), (II) or (III) is                new.
   R1, R3, R4, R8, R9, R10, R12 and R13 = 1-6C                alkyl;
   R2 = H or 1-6C alkyl;
   R5 = 1-6C alkyl, 1-4C alkoxy substituted 1-6C                alkyl group, oxygen-containing heterocyclic                substituted 1-6C alkyl group or aryl group;
   R6 and R7 = H or 1-3C alkyl;
   R11 = H, benzyl or phenyl-substituted allyl;                and
   R14 = 1-6C ester group, sulfonyl or                phosphonate.
   An INDEPENDENT CLAIM is also included for                preparation of (A).
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preparation (Claimed): The preparation of                  (A), comprises adding allyl alcohol amine formate                  based compound, electron-deficient olefin,                  transition metal salt catalyst and ligand and                  oxidant to organic solvent, heating and reacting                  under argon atmosphere to obtain (A). Preferred                  Method: The volume of organic solvent is 2-8                  l/mol based on the amount of allyl alcohol amine                  formate based compound, the heating temperature                  is 50-100 degrees C, and the reaction time is                  10-20 hours. Preferred Components: The allyl                  alcohol amine formate based compound is allyl                  alcohol or cyclohexene 3-ol derivative carbamate.                  The electron deficient olefin is acrylate,                  alkenyl sulfone or alkenyl phosphonate. The                  transition metal salt catalyst is palladium salt,                  the palladium salt is palladium acetate,                  palladium chloride or palladium trifluoroacetate.                  The ligand is amino acid, and the amino acid is                  N-acetylglycine-glycine or N-acetylisoleucine.                  The oxidant is a silver salt, and the silver salt                  is silver acetate or silver carbonate. The ratio                  between allyl alcohol amine formate based                  compound, electron-deficient olefin, ligand                  transition metal salt catalyst and oxidant is                  1:1-3:0.1-0.3:0.1-0.2:2-4. The organic solvent is                  N,N-dimethylformamide, dimethyl sulfoxide,                  tetrahydrofuran, 1,4-dioxane, 1,2-dichloroethane,                  acetonitrile, hexafluoroisopropanol or                  trifluoroethanol.
