   EASTMAN CHEM CO (EACH-C)
   EASTMAN CHEM CO (EACH-C)
   EASTMAN CHEM CO (EACH-C)
AB    NOVELTY - Production of carboxylic acid composition                involves oxidizing in a oxidation zone on at least                one oxidizable compound in the presence of an                oxidizing gas comprising oxygen and a solvent                stream, and a catalyst system at a temperature to                produce a reaction medium comprising                5-(alkoxycarbonyl)furan-2-carboxylic acid (ACFC).                The oxidation zone comprises at least one oxidation                reactor. The oxidizable compound containing methyl                5-methylfuran-2-carboxylate (MMFC), methyl                5-(hydroxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, methyl                5-(methoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, methyl                5-(ethoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, ethyl                5-methylfuran-2-carboxylate, ethyl                5-(hydroxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, ethyl                5-(methoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, ethyl                5-(ethoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, propyl                5-methylfuran-2-carboxylate,                propyl-5-(hydroxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate,                propyl-5-(methoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate,                propyl-5-(ethoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, and                isopropyl-5-methylfuran-2-carboxylate.
   USE - Production of carboxylic acid composition used                for producing variety of polyester product such as                poly(ethylene terephthalate) and its                copolymer.
   ADVANTAGE - The method enables efficient production                carboxylic acid composition with high yield.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Production of carboxylic acid composition                involves oxidizing in a oxidation zone on at least                one oxidizable compound in the presence of an                oxidizing gas comprising oxygen and a solvent                stream, and a catalyst system at a temperature to                produce a reaction medium comprising                5-(alkoxycarbonyl)furan-2-carboxylic acid (ACFC).                The oxidation zone comprises at least one oxidation                reactor. The oxidizable compound is at least one                chosen from methyl 5-methylfuran-2-carboxylate                (MMFC), methyl                5-(hydroxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, methyl                5-(methoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, methyl                5-(ethoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, ethyl                5-methylfuran-2-carboxylate, ethyl                5-(hydroxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, ethyl                5-(methoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, ethyl                5-(ethoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, propyl                5-methylfuran-2-carboxylate, propyl                5-(hydroxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, propyl                5-(methoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, propyl                5-(ethoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, isopropyl                5-methylfuran-2-carboxylate, isopropyl                5-(hydroxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, isopropyl                5-(methoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, methyl                5-((formyloxy)methyl)furan-2-carboxylate, methyl                5-(acetoxymethyl)furan-2-cayboxylate, methyl                5-((propionyloxy)methyl)furan-2-carboxylate, ethyl                5-((formyloxy)methyl)furan-2-carboxylate, ethyl                5-(acetoxymethyl)furan-2-cayboxylate, ethyl                5-((propionyloxy)methyl)furan-2-carboxylate, propyl                5-((formyloxy)methyl)furan-2-carboxylate, propyl                5-(acetoxymethyl)furan-2-cayboxylate, propyl                5-((propionyloxy)methyl)furan-2-carboxylate,                isopropyl 5-((formyloxy)methyl)furan-2-carboxylate,                isopropyl 5-(acetoxymethyl)furan-2-cayboxylate,                isopropyl                5-((propionyloxy)methyl)furan-2-carboxylate, or                isopropyl 5-(ethoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate to                generate a carboxylic acid composition comprising                ACFC.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - CHEMICAL ENGINEERING - Preferred Apparatus: The oxidation reactor                  comprises bubble column.
   TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - INORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The catalyst system                  comprises cobalt, manganese, bromide, nickel,                  and/or zirconium, preferably cobalt and                  manganese. Preferred Composition: The catalyst                  system comprises 2-100 ppm cobalt, 2-10000 ppm                  manganese and 2-10000 bromine with respect to the                  weight of the liquid in the reaction medium. The                  catalyst comprises 100-4500 ppm cobalt, 100-4500                  ppm manganese, and 100-45600 ppm bromine with                  respect to the weight of liquid in the oxidation                  zone. Preferred Condition: The oxidizing is                  conducted at 50-220&#176; C, preferably                  110-160&#176; C, and pressure of 50-1000                  psig.
   TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The oxidizable                  compound preferably comprises MMFC, methyl                  5-(hydroxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, methyl                  5-(methoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate, or methyl                  5-(ethoxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate. The                  carboxylic acid composition comprises ACFC and                  furan-2,5-dicarboxylic acid (FDCA). Preferred                  Properties: The ACFC is produced at yield of more                  than 70%, preferably 90%. The carboxylic acid                  composition has b* value of less than 20,                  preferably less than 1.

AB    NOVELTY - Producing carboxylic acid composition (110),                comprises oxidizing at least one oxidizable                compound in an oxidation zone (125) and in the                presence of an oxidizing gas (10) comprising oxygen                and a solvent stream (20) and a catalyst system at                a temperature to produce a reaction medium                comprising 5-(alkoxycarbonyl)furan-2-carboxylic                acids (ACFC), where the oxidation zone comprises at                least one oxidation reactor. The oxidizable                compound comprises e.g. methyl                5-methylfuran-2-carboxylate (MMFC), methyl                5-(hydroxymethyl)furan-2-carboxylate and/or                isopropyl 5-methylfuran-2-carboxylate.
   USE - The process is useful for producing carboxylic                acid composition.
   ADVANTAGE - The process: provides ACFC with an yield of                greater than 70%, preferably greater than 90%;                provides the carboxylic acid composition that has a                b* value of less than 20, preferably less than 1,                hence efficient (high yields) and low color; and                includes liquid phase oxidation that minimizes                solvent and starting material loss through carbon                burn, hence eco-friendly and economical.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Producing carboxylic acid composition (110),                comprising oxidizing at least one oxidizable                compound in an oxidation zone (125) and in the                presence of an oxidizing gas (10) comprising oxygen                and a solvent stream (20) and a catalyst system at                a temperature to produce a reaction medium                comprising 5-(alkoxycarbonyl)furan-2-carboxylic                acids (ACFC), where the oxidation zone comprises at                least one oxidation reactor. TI Catalyst for hydrodeoxygenating furfural and            5-hydroxymethylfurfural to methylated furans comprises            copper-nickel particles supported on titanium            dioxide.
AU WYMAN C E
   CAI C
   CHRISTOPHER P
   SEEMALA B
AE UNIV CALIFORNIA (REGC-C)
AB    NOVELTY - Catalyst comprises copper-nickel particles                supported on titanium dioxide, where the                copper-nickel particles form core-shell structures                in which copper is enriched at a surface of the                catalyst.
   USE - The catalyst is useful for hydrodeoxygenating                furfural and 5-hydroxymethylfurfural to methylated                furans (claimed).
   ADVANTAGE - The catalyst: enables high yield and                economically viable co-conversion of                5-hydroxymethylfurfural and furfural to methylated                furans and process simplicity; is low costs.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are also included                for:
   ynthesizing (M1) a catalyst for                hydrodeoxygenating furfural and                5-hydroxymethylfurfural to methylated furans,                comprising synthesizing monometallic copper                catalysts and monometallic nickel catalysts, mixing                the monometallic copper and nickel catalysts in                deionized water to form a bimetallic copper-nickel                catalyst, drying the mixture of the bimetallic                copper-nickel catalyst, calcining the dried mixture                of the bimetallic copper-nickel catalyst and                synthesizing the bimetallic copper-nickel catalyst                onto titanium dioxide to form a                copper-nickel/titanium dioxide catalyst, where the                copper-nickel particles form core-shell structures                in which copper is enriched at a surface of the                catalyst; and
   converting (M2) biomass into a fuel additive,                comprising liquefying the biomass to form a liquor,                neutralizing the liquor, precipitating lignin out                of the liquor, extracting furfural and                5-hydroxymethylfurfural from the liquor and                hydrodeoxygenating the extracted furfurals over a                copper-nickel/titanium dioxide catalyst.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - INORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The concentration of                  nickel at the surface of the catalyst is reduced,                  thus allowing the nickel to promote copper                  reactivity without compromising selectivity. The                  copper-nickel particles are 2.5-20 wt.% copper                  and 0.5-20 wt.% copper-nickel. The copper-nickel                  particles are 2.5 wt.% copper-0.5 wt.% nickel,                  preferably 20 wt % copper-20 wt.% nickel. The                  biomass is wood chips, and the fuel is                  blendstocks 2-methylfuran or 2,5-dimethylfuran.                  The acid catalyst is iron(III) chloride. The                  aqueous tetrahydrofuran comprises a                  tetrahydrofuran and water volume ratio of 4-1,                  where the water contains 1 wt % iron(III)                  chloride relative to a liquid phase. The ratio of                  the tetrahydrofuran to water is 1:1-7:1. The                  organic solvent contains at least one of                  methanol, ethanol, butanol, isopropyl alcohol,                  butanediol, pentanediol, toluene, dioxane,                  methyl-tetrahydrofuran, tetrahydrofuran, hexane,                  decane, nonane or methyl-isobutylketone. The                  liquid containing extracted furfural and                  5-hydroxymethylfurfural is hydrodeoxygenated to                  methylated furans. The copper-nickel particles                  are 2.5-20 wt.% copper and 0.5-20 wt.% nickel.                  Preferred Method: The liquefying the biomass to                  form the liquor comprises treating the biomass                  with a composition comprising a co-solvent                  mixture of aqueous tetrahydrofuran and an acid                  catalyst. The biomass is wood chips, the method                  further comprises: solubilizing the wood chips in                  a mixture of tetrahydrofuran with water                  containing iron(III) chloride, where the C5 and                  C6 sugars within the wood chips are                  simultaneously hydrolyzed to monomers and then                  co-dehydrated to fuel precursors furfural and                  hydroxylmethylfurfural, respectively. The                  neutralizing the liquor comprises: adding calcium                  dihydroxide, calcium carbonate, or ammonium                  hydroxide to the liquor to neutralize the acidic                  moieties and precipitate iron hydroxide ions and                  a portion of lignin; removing tetrahydrofuran                  from the liquor to precipitate a remaining                  portion of lignin; extracting furfural and                  5-hydroxymethylfurfural from the liquor using an                  organic solvent treated with calcium hydroxide;                  adding between 0.001 g and 0.1 g of calcium                  hydroxide per 25 mL of to the extracted organic                  solvent liquid. The copper-nickel/titanium                  dioxide catalyst comprises: copper-nickel                  particles supported on titanium dioxide, where                  the copper-nickel particles form core-shell                  structures in which copper is enriched at a                  surface of the catalyst, and a concentration of                  nickel at the surface of the catalyst is reduced,                  thus allowing the nickel to promote copper                  reactivity without compromising                  selectivity.

   EXAMPLE - Over a solution of 100 mg                1-methyl-N-(2-methyl-4-(6-(trifluoromethyl)quinolin-2-yl)phenyl)-1H-pyrazole-5-carboxamide                in 1 ml dimethylformamide, 9.8 mg sodium hydride                was added. The resulting solution was stirred for                30 minutes at room temperature. 34.6 mg methyl                iodide was added and the reaction mixture stirred                for an additional horn at room temperature. The                reaction was quenched with a saturated solution of                20 ml ammonium chloride and extracted with (3/*20                ml) ethyl acetate. The organic layers were                combined, concentrated under reduced pressure and                purified to give 69%                N,1-dimethyl-N-(2-methyl-4-(6-(trifluoromethyl)quinolin-2-yl)phenyl)-1H-pyrazole-5-carboxamide                as a white solid.

The amount of transition metal element                added is 3-30 %mass. The molar ratio of transition                metal element and nitrogen element is (0.1-1):1.                The amount of transition metal compound is 20-40                times the sum of the mass of nitrogen-containing                compounds.
   USE - Catalyst for catalytic hydrogenation to                produce alkane e.g. 18C alkane used for preparing                biofuel (claimed).
   ADVANTAGE - The catalyst has high catalytic                activity.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - An INDEPENDENT CLAIM is included for                preparation of catalyst for catalytic hydrogenation                to produce alkane, which involves adding transition                metal compound, nitrogen-containing compound and                carrier to solvent, stirring, evaporating to                dryness and calcining the under protection of inert                gas at 300-1100 degrees C for 2-8 hours.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - INORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The transition metal                  is iron or cobalt. The transition metal compound                  is organic salt of transition metal or inorganic                  salt of transition metal. Preferably, the                  transition metal compound is transition metal                  acetate, acetylacetonate, nitrate or chloride.                  The carrier is activated carbon, aluminum oxide,                  zirconium dioxide, titanium dioxide, silicon                  dioxide or cerium oxide. Preferred Composition:                  The content of transition metal element is 10-20                  %mass. The molar ratio of transition metal                  element and nitrogen element is 1:3. Preferred                  Conditions: The stirring temperature is                  controlled at 40-80 degrees C for 8-24 hours. The                  drying temperature is controlled at 50-80 degrees                  C for 12-16 hours. The calcination temperature is                  controlled at 550-900 degrees C for 3-6 hours.                  The stirring temperature is controlled at 40-80                  degrees C for 8-24 hours. The drying temperature                  is controlled at 50-80 degrees C for 12-16 hours.                  The calcination temperature is controlled at                  550-900 degrees C for 3-6 hours.
   TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The                  nitrogen-containing compound is dinitramine,                  melamine or phenanthroline (1,10-phenanthroline).                  The solvent is ethanol or water. The organic                  solvent is saturated linear liquid alkane. The                  organic solvent has saturated linear alkane                  having carbon number of 6-14. Preferred                  Composition: The mass ratio of catalyst for                  catalytic hydrogenation to produce alkane and the                  fatty acid or fatty acid ester is (0.5-1):1. The                  amount of organic solvent is 20-100 times the sum                  of the mass of the catalyst and the reactant. The                  organic solvent has saturated linear alkane                  having carbon number of 8-12. The amount of                  organic solvent is 40-80 times the sum of the                  mass of the catalyst and the reactant. Preferred                  Conditions: The reaction temperature of                  hydrodeoxygenation reaction is controlled at                  280-350 degrees C and hydrogen pressure of 40-60                  atmospheres.

PN WO2018216030-A1; WO2018216030-A9; IN201711018555-A; EP3630739-A1; US2021147374-A1; US11136302-B2; IN411782-B
TI Synthesizing furan derivative e.g.            2,5-di(formyl)furan i.e. used for synthesis of            polymers, antifungal agents, drugs and ligands,            involves stirring reaction mixture of carbohydrate in            solvent in presence of catalyst.
AU RODE C V
   SHINDE S H
AE COUNCIL SCI & IND RES INDIA (COUI-C)
   COUNCIL SCI & IND RES INDIA (COUI-C)
   COUNCIL SCI & IND RES INDIA (COUI-C)
   COUNCIL SCI & IND RES INDIA (COUI-C)
AB    NOVELTY - Single step, single pot process for                synthesizing furan derivative from carbohydrate                comprises stirring the reaction mixture of                carbohydrate in solvent in presence of catalyst at                170-190 degrees C for 23-25 hours to form                corresponding furan derivative.
   USE - The process is useful for synthesizing furan                derivative from carbohydrate, where the furan                derivative is 2,5-di(formyl)furan or                5-((methylthio)methyl)-2-furfural (claimed), and                the 2,5-di(formyl)furan is used in the synthesis of                polymers, antifungal agents, drugs and ligands and                to produce unsubstituted furan.
   ADVANTAGE - The process: provides furan derivative at an                yield of 30-60%, preferably 30-50%; is performed                using a single catalyst and single solvent, hence                simple and economical; does not require external                oxygen for 2,5-di(formyl)furan production; does not                require external source of sulfur for                5-((methylthio)methyl)-2-furfural production; does                not require external oxygen pressure; and does not                require isolation of                5-(hydroxymethyl)furfural.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - INORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The catalyst is                  sulfuric acid or tin hydroxide                  nanoparticles-embedded montmorillonite.
   TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The solvent is                  dimethyl sulfoxide, N,N-dimethylformamide, water,                  and/or 1-butyl-3-methylimidazolium                  chloride.
   TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - POLYMERS - Preferred Components: The carbohydrate is                  fructose, glucose or sucrose.
   EXAMPLE - Solution of 10 g fructose in 10 ml dimethyl                sulfoxide was heated at 180 degrees C for 24 hours,                under stirring in the presence of 4 g tin hydroxide                nanoparticles-embedded montmorillonite. Because                small quantities of decomposition products of                dimethyl sulfoxide (unpleasant odor) were produced                during the reaction, the outgoing gas was bubbled                through bleach (sodium hypochlorite) to oxidize                dimethyl sulfide and fully destroy the odor. Once                the highest yield of                5-((methylthio)methyl)-2-furfural was achieved, the                reaction mixture was cooled to room temperature and                filtered to separate the catalyst. Catalyst bed was                washed with 300 ml dichloromethane further mother                liquor was washed with water (2x 100 ml). Separated                organic phase was evaporated and passed through                silica (60-120 mesh size) to yield 3.29 g pure                5-((methylthio)methyl)-2-furfural.

PN WO2018189157-A1
TI Converting a carboxylic acid comprises using a            diboron reagent to convert a carboxylic acid or imide            ester derivatives to a boronic ester.
AU AGGARWAL V
   FAWCETT A
   MYERS E
AE UNIV BRISTOL (UYBS-C)
AB    NOVELTY - Converting (M1) a carboxylic acid comprises                using a diboron reagent to convert a carboxylic                acid or imide ester derivatives to a boronic                ester.
   USE - The methods are useful for converting                carboxylic acid or imide ester derivative of a                carboxylic acid to a boronic ester (all                claimed).
   ADVANTAGE - The methods facilitate bioisosteric lead                optimization and thus provide boronic esters with                high yield by direct conversion of carboxylic acid                or imide ester derivative of carboxylic acid.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are also included                for:
   converting (M2) a carboxylic acid, comprising                providing an imide ester of a carboxylic acid and                treating the imide ester with a diboron reagent,                thus forming a boronic ester;
   converting (M3) a carboxylic acid, comprising                providing a phthalimide ester of a carboxylic acid,                which is a derivative of N-hydroxyphthalimide or                N-hydroxytetrachlorophthalimide, and treating the                phthalimide ester with a diboron reagent, thus                forming a boronic ester of the carboxylic acid;                and
   preparing a boronic acid or a boronic ester                comprising the capture of an alkyl radical with a                diboron reagent.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The diboron reagent:                  comprises bis(catecholato)diboron and/or                  diboronic acid. The diboron reagent: further                  comprises 0.1-0.9 equivalent preferably 1.1-2                  equivalent catechol; and comprises                  bis(catecholato)diboron and/or diboronic acid in                  the presence of catechol. The imide ester is a                  phthalimide ester, or a phthalimide ester derived                  from N-hydroxyphthalimide or halogen-substituted                  N-hydroxyphthalimide, preferably                  N-hydroxytetrachlorophthalimide. Preferred                  Method: The methods (M1) and (M2) are conducted:                  in a solvent, preferably an amide solvent                  comprising dimethylacetamide, dimethylformamide,                  N,N'-dimethylpropyleneurea,                  1,3-dimethyl-2-imidazolidinone,                  N-methyl-2-pyrrolidone and tetramethylurea; and                  in the presence of an additive, preferably at                  least one of 4- methoxypyridine,                  4-dimethylaminopyridine, pyridine,                  1,4-diazabicyclo[2.2.2]octane,                  1,8-diazabicyclo[5.4.0]undec-7-ene, quinuclidine,                  1,1,3,3-tetramethylguanidine,                  triazabicyclodecene,                  1,3,4,6,7,8-hexahydro-1-methyl-2H-pyrimido[1,2-a]pyrimidine,                  2-picoline, 4-picoline, 3-picoline, imidazole,                  potassium carbonate, acetic acid, triethylamine,                  4-cyanopyridine or 4-phenylpyridine. Providing an                  imide ester comprises a step of reacting an                  N-hydroxyimide with the carboxylic acid, or                  esterifying N-hydroxyphthalimide or                  halogen-substituted N-hydroxyphthalimide,                  preferably N-hydroxytetrachlorophthalimide with                  the carboxylic acid. The method (M1) further                  comprises a subsequent step of converting the                  boronic ester produced by the method into another                  boronic ester, where converting the boronic ester                  comprises ligand exchange of the boronic ester,                  preferably with a ligand composition comprising                  pinacol and/or N-methyliminodiacetic acid (MIDA).                  Treating the imide ester with the diboron reagent                  is conducted under illumination with light having                  a component with a wavelength 410-480 nm or there                  is no illumination other than, optionally ambient                  light. The method (M3) further comprises                  converting the boronic ester by ligand exchange                  of the boronic ester, preferably by treatment                  with a ligand composition comprising pinacol                  and/or MIDA.
   EXAMPLE - 500 mg Carboxylic acid coupled with                N-hydroxyphthalimide (NHPI) was added to a                round-bottomed flask. 0.1 M dichloromethane and, if                liquid, 1 equivalent carboxylic acid was then                added, followed by 1 equivalent                diisopropylcarbodiimide. The reaction mixture was                allowed to stir at room temperature overnight,                before concentrating under reduced pressure. The                crude residue was purified by flash column                chromatography to give 87% NHPI ester 60 as                colorless oil. The 38 mg NHPI 60 ester and 40 mg                bis(catecholato)diboron were carefully weighed into                a flame-dried 7 ml vial containing a small magnetic                stirrer bar. 1.3 ml dimethylacetamide was added and                then the headspace of the vial was purged with a                gentle stream of argon for 10 seconds. The vial was                tightly sealed and stirred under blue LED                irradiation for 14 hours. Then 63 mg pinacol was                dissolved in 0.45 ml triethylamine, added to the                reaction mixture and stirred for 1 hour. For                workup, the reaction mixture was transferred into a                vial containing 15 ml ethyl acetate, 3 ml water, 3                ml saturated aqueous solution of ammonium chloride.                After vigorously shaking and allowing the two                layers to separate, the top organic layer was                carefully removed with a pipette. This was repeated                twice more with 5 ml ethyl acetate. The organic                layers were combined and concentrated under reduced                pressure. The crude residue was directly purified                to yield 57% boronic ester.

After completion of the reaction, the reaction mixture was diluted with 20 ml water and extracted with 2x 75 ml ethyl alcohol. The obtained organic extracts were combined and washed with 30 ml brine, dried over anhydrous sodium sulfate, filtered, and concentrated in vacuum to give (S)-2,9-dimethyl-3-phenyl-6-((1-(3-vinylphenyl)cyclopentyl)methyl)-3,4-dihydro-1H-pyrazin(1,2-c)pyrimidine-1,8(2H)-dione (E1). Then mixture of 110 mg E1 and 10% 25 mg palladium on carbon (wet) in 15 ml ethyl acetate was stirred at 20-25 degrees C for 3.5 hours under hydrogen. The reaction mixture was filtered and the filtrate was concentrated under reduced pressure to give (S)-9-(benzyloxy)-6-((1-(3-ethylphenyl)cyclopentyl)methyl)-2-methyl-3-phenyl-3,4-dihydro-1H-pyrazino(1,2-c)pyrimidine-1,8(2H)-dione (E2). To a stirred solution of 110 mg (E2) in 6 ml methanol, 6 ml concentrated aqueous hydrochloric acid was added at 0 degrees C and the reaction mixture was stirred for 20 hours at 8-17 degrees C. After completion of the reaction, the solvent was concentrated and ice was added, the reaction mixture was extracted with 10% methanol in 50x 2 ml dichloromethane, the resulting organic layer was dried and concentrated. The obtained crude product was purified to give 22.8 mg (S)-6-((1-(3,5-dimethylphenyl)cyclopentyl)methyl)-9-hydroxy-2-methyl-3-(((S)-tetrahydrofuran-2-yl)methyl)-3,4-dihydro-1H-pyrazino(1,2-c)pyrimidine-1,8(2H)-dione(24.7% yield).

TI Preparing monophenol compounds by ammonia complex            catalyzed lignin degradation comprises mixing lignin,            base, peroxide, metal salt, ammonia solution and            deionized water to form clear solution, and heating to            obtain degradation product.
AU LI Y
   DU F
   LI B
   XIAN X
   CHEN D
AE GUANGXI ACAD SCI (GXSC-C)
AB    NOVELTY - Preparing monophenol compounds by ammonia                complex catalyzed lignin degradation comprises (i)                mixing lignin, base, peroxide, metal salt, ammonia                solution and deionized water, mixing to form a                clear solution; (ii) stirring, heating, natural                cooling to room temperature to obtain a degradation                product; (iii) adjusting the pH of the primary                degradation product, heating, reacting, naturally                cooling to obtain secondary degradation products;                and (iv) decomposing the secondary degradation                product, collecting filtrate, extracting with                dichloromethane, separating the organic phase and                drying.
   USE - The method is useful for preparing monophenol                compounds by ammonia complex catalyzed lignin                degradation (claimed).
   ADVANTAGE - The method increases the contact site of the                catalyst and the lignin aqueous solution, has mild                reaction condition, can efficiently catalyze the                degradation of lignin, has high efficiency and low                cost and is suitable for large-scale industrial                production.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Preparing monophenol compounds by ammonia                complex catalyzed lignin degradation comprises (i)                mixing1-10 pts. lignin, 0.5-1.5 pts. base, 0.5-16                pts. peroxide, 0.1-0.25 pts. metal salt, 1-5 pts.                ammonia solution and 100 pts. deionized water,                adding into high pressure reactor, quickly sealing,                high speed mixing to 300 revolutions/minute to form                a clear solution; (ii) stirring to 300                revolutions/minute, heating in a high-pressure                reactor to 120-180 degrees C for 100-240 minutes,                natural cooling to room temperature to obtain a                degradation product; (iii) adjusting the pH of the                primary degradation product to 2-3 with an acid,                sealing and adding into high pressure reactor,                heating to 100-120 degrees C, reacting for 10-20                minutes, naturally cooling to room temperature to                obtain secondary degradation products; and (iv)                decomposing the secondary degradation product under                reduced pressure, collecting the filtrate,                extracting the filtrate with dichloromethane,                separating the organic phase and drying with                anhydrous calcium chloride, distilling off the                dichloromethane under reduced pressure to obtain                phenol mixed solution.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - INORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The lignin is bagasse                  lignin or eucalyptus lignin. The metal salt is                  zinc acetate, zinc oxide, zinc sulfate, zinc                  chloride, zinc nitrate, zinc carbonate, zinc                  hydroxide, zinc sulfide and zinc oxalate. The                  peroxide agent in step (1) is a mixture of 30                  mass% aqueous hydrogen peroxide solution and 5                  mass% aqueous solution of zinc hypochlorite. The                  base is sodium hydroxide, potassium hydroxide or                  lithium hydroxide. The ammonia solution is 5-35                  mass% aqueous ammonia solution. The acid is 10-15                  mass% aqueous sulfuric acid solution, 30-45 wt.%                  aqueous acetic acid solution and 15-25 wt.%                  aqueous hydrochloric acid solution.

The amount of organic                  solvent is 100-1000 times the amount of                  5-hydroxymethylfurfural. Preferred Method: The                  method for preparing heterogeneous iron-based                  catalyst comprises mixing iron compound and                  nitrogen-containing compound, then adding                  ethanol, and dissolving with stirring, and adding                  carrier, heating the reaction, removing ethanol                  after completion of reaction, drying, and                  calcinating at 400-1000&#176; C, preferably                  800&#176; C under inert gas atmosphere to                  obtain heterogeneous iron-based catalyst, then                  adding carrier, heating the reaction to 60oC for                  15 hours, where inert gas is argon gas. The                  method for recovering heterogeneous iron-based                  catalyst for re-using includes filtering reaction                  solution of step (ii), centrifuging, and                  recovering heterogeneous iron-based                  catalyst.

TI Producing tetrahydrofuran and 1,4-butanediol from            furan, comprises contacting furan with hydrogen and            water in presence of catalytic composition, stopping            flow of furan and water, subjecting composition to gas            stream and re-starting flow.
AU HAAN R J
   LANGE J P A M J G
   LANGE J P A M J
   HAAN R Y
AE SHELL INT RES MIJ BV (SHEL-C)
   SHELL OIL CO (SHEL-C)
   SHELL INT RES MIJ BV (SHEL-C)
   SHELL OIL CO (SHEL-C)
   SHELL OIL CO (SHEL-C)
   SHELL INT RES MIJ BV (SHEL-C)
AB    NOVELTY - Producing tetrahydrofuran and 1,4-butanediol                from furan in presence of catalytic composition                containing metal of iron, ruthenium, osmium,                cobalt, rhenium, iridium, nickel, palladium or                platinum and optionally containing additional metal                on solid support, comprises: (a1) contacting furan                with hydrogen and water in reactor in presence of                the composition for time; (a2) stopping flow of                furan and water to reactor and removing furan and                water; subjecting composition to gas stream                comprising hydrogen at 200-600&#176; C in                absence of furan and water; and re-starting flow of                furan and water to reactor.
   USE - The process is useful for producing                tetrahydrofuran and 1,4-butanediol.
   ADVANTAGE - The process: allows for a simpler and faster                regeneration of the catalytic composition; and                contains fewer process steps. In the process: no                additional equipment is required for the                regeneration, in comparison with oxidative                regeneration which requires such equipment as                dedicated pipelines with valves, flow meters,                mixers and controllers to feed air (or air diluted                with nitrogen) for regeneration and to accommodate                the effluent; neither any additional gas tanks for                nitrogen and air nor air blowers (with                purification) are needed.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - INORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The catalyst contains                  at least one additional metal comprising rhenium.                  The solid support is titanium oxides, zirconium                  oxides or active carbon.
   TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Process: The furan is contacted                  with hydrogen and water in a reactor in the                  presence of the catalytic composition for the                  time it takes for the activity of the catalytic                  composition to be reduced by at least 5% and at                  most 90%, as compared to the original activity of                  the catalytic composition under the same                  conditions. The temperature at which the                  catalytic composition is subjected to a gas                  stream comprising hydrogen in step (a3) is at                  least 25&#176; C higher than the temperature                  at which furan is contacted with hydrogen and                  water in a reactor in the presence of the                  catalytic composition for a time. The temperature                  at which furan is contacted with hydrogen and                  water in a reactor in the presence of the                  catalytic composition for 100-350&#176; C.                  The temperature at which the catalytic                  composition is subjected to a gas stream                  comprising hydrogen in step (a3) is                  250-450&#176; C. The reactor is cooled prior                  to re-starting the flow of furan and the flow of                  water to the reactor. The process of steps (a1)                  to (a4) are repeated more than once with the same                  catalytic composition.
   EXAMPLE - Catalyst was evaluated in a four-barrel                microflow unit that consists of 4 parallel                Hastelloy HC 276 reactor . The reactors had an                isothermal zone of 25 cm length and an internal                volume of 41 ml. The reactors can be operated at                40-500&#176; C under 1.5-140 bar pressure. The                liquid feed was fed to the reactor by 1000 ml pumps                with a maximum flow rate of 100 ml/hour. Hydrogen                was applied to the reactor through a mass flow                controller with a maximum flow rate of 5 nl/hour.                The catalysts were loaded as crushed (30-80 mesh)                particles, as 3 g load, and diluted in an equal                weight of silicon carbide (0.2 mm) . The catalysts                in each reactor consisted of an active carbon that                was impregnated with 4 wt.% rhenium and 0.04 wt.%                palladium. The initial catalyst reduction was                carried at 275&#176; C for 16 hours under                atmospheric pressure and 1 nl/h flow of 50 vol.%                hydrogen in nitrogen and, subsequently, for 2 hours                at 4 bar and a 1 nl/h flow of pure 100% hydrogen.                After reduction the temperature was lowered to                200&#176; C, the hydrogen flow and pressure                were set to target and the furan-containing liquid                flow was admitted to the reactor. The reaction was                then carried out over a wide operation window for                some 900 hours. The window covered temperatures of                130-200&#176; C, pressures of 30-130bar, WHSV                (sic) of 0.2-2/h, and feed concentrations of 12-30                wt.% for furan and 27-34 w% for water with ethanol                as balance. After some 900 hours on stream, the                catalyst was subjected to a regeneration step that                consists of stopping the liquid feed, draining the                reactor for 30 minutes, raising the reactor                temperature to 400&#176; C within 10 hours,                maintaining it at 400&#176; C for 4 hours,                cooling back to 150&#176; C within 16 hours and                resuming the liquid feed under reference                conditions.

The                oxidation process is carried out continuously in                flow, where there are provided means for varying                reaction parameters, the solvent for the oxidation                process is water and a dipolar aprotic solvent is                present as a co-solvent.
   USE - The process is useful for selective production                of oxidized furan derivatives, where the oxidized                furan derivative is                5-hydroxymethylfuran-2-carboxylic acid,                2,5-diformylfuran, 5-formylfuran-2-carboxylic acid,                and 2,5-furandicarboxylic acid (all claimed).
   ADVANTAGE - The process is safe, fast, environmentally                friendly and economical.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Process for the selective production of                oxidized furan derivatives, comprises reacting                5-hydroxymethyl-2-furfural of formula (I) in the                presence of a solvent, an oxidation agent, a                catalyst, and optionally a base and/or a                co-solvent. The oxidation process is carried out                continuously in flow, where there are provided                means for varying reaction parameters, the solvent                for the oxidation process is water and a dipolar                aprotic solvent is present as a co-solvent.
                                                                                                                     
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - INORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The platinum on                  activated charcoal is used as the catalyst. The                  oxidation agent is compressed oxygen or                  compressed air.
   TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Process: In the process, the                  reaction parameters are temperature, pressure,                  oxidation agent, and/or catalyst. In the process,                  the reaction temperature is 50-180&#176; C,                  preferably 60-160&#176; C, the working                  pressure is 5-100 bar, preferably 10-80 bar. The                  reaction temperature for the production of:                  5-hydroxymethylfuran-2-carboxylic acid is                  60-120&#176; C, preferably 100-120&#176;                  C; 2,5-diformylfuran is 100-160&#176; C,                  preferably 140-160&#176; C;                  5-formylfuran-2-carboxylic acid is                  60-160&#176; C, preferably 100-120&#176;                  C; 2,5-furandicarboxylic acid is 60-160&#176;                  C, preferably 80-120&#176; c. In the process,                  a stream enriched with 5-hydroxymethyl-2-furfural                  from previous dehydration reactions, preferably                  dehydrations of sugars, is employed as a starting                  material. In the process, the selective                  production of 2,5-furandicarboxylic acid starting                  from 5-hydroxymethyl-2-furfural, characterized by                  the combination of: a base including carbonates                  and bicarbonates, preferably sodium carbonate                  and/or sodium bicarbonate is used as a                  co-catalyst; and the working pressure of 80-100                  bar, where the temperature is 120-160&#176;                  C, preferably 140-160&#176; C. Preferred                  Components: The co-solvent is                  N-methylpyrrolidone. The oxidized furan                  derivative comprises at least one aldehyde group                  and/or at least one carboxylic acid group. The                  catalyst used to obtain: 2,5-diformylfuran is                  potassium-cryptomelane type crystalline                  mixed-valent manganese (oxide)-based octahedral                  molecular sieve(s) (K-OMS-2);                  5-hydroxymethylfuran-2-carboxylic acid,                  5-formylfuran-2-carboxylic acid and                  2,5-furandicarboxylic acid is platinum on                  activated charcoal. The base is used as a                  co-catalyst. The base is hydroxide, carbonate or                  bicarbonate, including alkali hydroxide, alkali                  carbonate or alkali bicarbonate, preferably                  sodium hydroxide, sodium carbonate or sodium                  bicarbonate.
   EXAMPLE - CatCart(RTM: Catalyst cartridge system) was                filled first with 20 mg of Celite 545(RTM:                Diatomaceous earth, flux-calcined) and then 280 mg                10% Platinum on carbon were added. Fresh                CatCart(RTM: Catalyst cartridge system) was used                every time, when the system pressure was changed.                Before each screening series, the entire reaction                line was purged with water, the Teflon(RTM:                Polytetrafluoroethylene liner) frit of the system                valve was replaced and ThalesNano X-cube system                self-test was performed. The initial system                stabilization was always achieved using sodium                hydroxide/water solution and when the reaction                parameters remained constant, the pumping of the                reaction solution began, then the system was                allowed to stabilize and equilibrate at the new                conditions for 10 minutes and two samples of 1 ml                each were then collected. Then the temperature was                increased and the system was again allowed to                stabilize. In the selective oxidation of                5-hydroxymethyl-2-furfural to                5-hydroxymethylfuran-2-carboxylic acid,                temperature-mediated catalyst deactivation was used                to synthesize 5-hydroxymethylfuran-2-carboxylic                acid in favor of the fully oxidized                2,5-furandicarboxylic acid.

AB    NOVELTY - Producing a terminal hydroxyl (C3-8                hydroxyalkyl)-C(=O)OCH3 ester in a recombinant host                comprises (a) enzymatically converting a C4-9                carboxylic acid to a (C3-8 alkyl)-C(=O)OCH3 ester                using a polypeptide having fatty acid                O-methyltransferase activity; and (b) enzymatically                converting the (C3-8 alkyl)-C(=O)OCH3 ester to                terminal hydroxyl (C3-8 hydroxyalkyl)-C(=O)OCH3                ester using a polypeptide having monooxygenase                activity.
   USE - The methods are used for producing a terminal                hydroxyl (C3-8 hydroxyalkyl)-C(=O)OCH3 ester and                terminal hydroxy-substituted (C4-9                alkyl)-OC(=O)-(C3-8 alkyl) esters in a recombinant                host; and enzymatically producing                5-hydroxypentanoate (all claimed).
   ADVANTAGE - Provided are sustainable methods for producing                glutaric acid, 5-hydroxypentanoate,                5-aminopentanoate, cadaverine and 1,5-pentanediol                (C5 building blocks) where the methods are                biocatalyst based.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Producing a terminal hydroxyl (C3-8                hydroxyalkyl)-C(=O)OCH3 ester in a recombinant host                comprises (a) enzymatically converting a C4-9                carboxylic acid to a (C3-8 alkyl)-C(=O)OCH3 ester                using a polypeptide having fatty acid                O-methyltransferase activity, the polypeptide                having fatty acid O-methyltransferase activity                having greater than or equal to 70% sequence                identity to a defined sequence of 368, 360, or 394                amino acids (SEQ ID NO. 23-25); and (b)                enzymatically converting the (C3-8 alkyl)-C(=O)OCH3                ester to terminal hydroxyl (C3-8                hydroxyalkyl)-C(=O)OCH3 ester using a polypeptide                having monooxygenase activity, where the                polypeptide having monooxygenase activity is                classified under EC 1.14.14.-or EC 1.14.15.- and                has \70% sequence identity to a defined sequence of                418, 420, 420, 402, or 523 amino acids (SEQ ID NO.                14-16, 28, or 29), the method optionally further                comprising enzymatically converting the terminal                hydroxyl (C3-8 hydroxyalkyl)-C(=O)OCH3 ester to a                terminal hydroxyl C4-9 hydroxyalkanoate. TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - BIOTECHNOLOGY - Preferred Method: In the method of producing                  a terminal hydroxyl (C3-8 hydroxyalkyl)-C(=O)OCH3                  ester in a recombinant host, the C4-9 carboxylic                  acid is enzymatically produced from a C4-9                  alkanoyl-CoA using: a polypeptide having                  thioesterase activity, where the polypeptide                  having thioesterase activity has greater than or                  equal to 70% sequence identity to a defined                  sequence of 286, 246, or 261 amino acids (SEQ ID                  NO. 1, 33, or 34), given in the specification; or                  a polypeptide having butanal dehydrogenase                  activity and a polypeptide having aldehyde                  dehydrogenase activity. A polypeptide having                  demethylase activity or a polypeptide having                  esterase activity enzymatically converts the                  terminal hydroxyl (C3-8 hydroxyalkyl)-C(=O)OCH3                  ester to a terminal hydroxyl C4-9                  hydroxyalkanoate. The C4-9 carboxylic acid is                  pentanoate, and is enzymatically converted to                  pentanoate methyl ester; and the pentanoate                  methyl ester is enzymatically converted to                  5-hydroxypentanoate methyl ester, the method                  optionally further comprising enzymatically                  converting 5-hydroxypentanoate methyl ester to                  5-hydroxypentanoate using a polypeptide having                  demethylase or esterase activity. The C4-9                  alkanoyl-CoA is pentanoyl-CoA and pentanoate is                  enzymatically produced from pentanoyl-CoA.                  Pentanoyl-CoA is produced from acetyl-CoA and                  propanoyl-CoA via two cycles of CoA-dependent                  carbon chain elongation, where each of the two                  cycles of CoA-dependent carbon chain elongation                  comprises using (i) a polypeptide having beta                  -ketothiolase activity or a polypeptide having                  acetyl-CoA carboxylase activity and a polypeptide                  having beta -ketoacyl-(acyl carrier protein                  (acp)) synthase activity, (ii) a polypeptide                  having 3-hydroxyacyl-CoA dehydrogenase activity                  or a polypeptide having 3-oxoacyl-CoA reductase                  activity, (iv) a polypeptide having enoyl-CoA                  hydratase activity, and (v) a polypeptide having                  trans-2-enoyl-CoA reductase activity to form                  pentanoyl-CoA from acetyl-CoA and propanoyl-CoA.                  The recombinant host is subjected to a                  cultivation strategy under aerobic, anaerobic or,                  micro-aerobic cultivation conditions. The                  recombinant host is cultured under conditions of                  nutrient limitation, such as phosphate, nitrogen                  and oxygen limitation. The recombinant host is                  retained using a ceramic membrane to maintain a                  high cell density during fermentation. The                  principal carbon source fed to the recombinant                  host derives from a biological feedstock, where                  the biological feedstock is, or derives from,                  monosaccharides, disaccharides, lignocellulose,                  hemicellulose, cellulose, lignin, levulinic acid,                  formic acid, triglycerides, glycerol, fatty                  acids, agricultural waste, condensed distillers'                  solubles, or municipal waste. The recombinant host is a                  prokaryote selected from Escherichia,                  Clostridium, Corynebacterium, Cupriavidus,                  Pseudomonas, Delftia, Bacillus, Lactobacillus,                  Lactococcus, and Rhodococcus, or a eukaryote                  selected from Aspergillus, Saccharomyces, Pichia,                  Yarrowia, Issatchenkia, Debaryomyces, Arxula, and                  Kluyveromyces. The recombinant host comprises the                  following attenuated enzymes: a polypeptide                  having polyhydroxyalkanoate synthase activity, a                  polypeptide having acetyl-CoA thioesterase                  activity, a polypeptide having propanoyl-CoA                  thioesterase activity, a polypeptide having                  phosphotransacetylase forming acetate activity, a                  polypeptide having acetate kinase activity, a                  polypeptide having lactate dehydrogenase                  activity, a polypeptide having                  menaquinol-fumarate oxidoreductase activity, a                  polypeptide having 2-oxoacid decarboxylase                  activity producing isobutanol, a polypeptide                  having alcohol dehydrogenase activity forming                  ethanol, a polypeptide having triose phosphate                  isomerase activity, a polypeptide having pyruvate                  decarboxylase activity, a polypeptide having                  glucose-6-phosphate isomerase activity, a                  polypeptide having transhydrogenase activity                  dissipating the cofactor gradient, a polypeptide                  having glutamate dehydrogenase activity specific                  for the cofactor gradient, a NADH/NADPH-utilizing                  polypeptide having glutamate dehydrogenase                  activity, a polypeptide having pimeloyl-CoA                  dehydrogenase activity; a polypeptide having                  acyl-CoA dehydrogenase activity accepting C5                  building blocks and central precursors as                  substrates; a polypeptide having glutaryl-CoA                  dehydrogenase activity; or a polypeptide having                  pimeloyl-CoA synthetase activity. AB    NOVELTY - Nanostructure comprises: a silver nanoparticle                core; a mesoporous silica shell; and a                photosensitizer.
   USE - The nanostructure is useful for killing                bacteria comprising Gram-positive and Gram-negative                bacteria (all claimed). Test details are described                but no results given.
   ADVANTAGE - The nanostructure: exhibits significant                enhancement in singlet oxygen production; display                good stability in singlet oxygen production over a                long illumination time, with no decay in activity;                does not require incubation time for the uptake of                the hybrid by the bacteria; and exhibits very good                efficiency in broad-spectrum photodynamic                inactivation of bacteria and synergistic effect.                Singlet oxygen production may increase linearly                with the increased concentration of hybrids,                indicating little self-quenching at higher                concentrations, and singlet oxygen production does                not linearly increase with the increased                concentrations of pure photosensitizers, which                indicates strong self-quenching at higher                concentrations, thus utilized in more applications                than pure photosensitizers.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - An INDEPENDENT CLAIM is also included for                killing bacteria comprising contacting bacteria                with a nanostructure comprising a silver                nanoparticle core, a mesoporous silica shell and a                photosensitizer to form a blend and exposing the                blend to light.
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The photosensitizer                  is: covalently bonded to the silver nanoparticle;                  adsorbed onto the mesoporous silica shell; and                  hematoporphyrin,                  meso-tetra(4-carboxyphenyl)porphine,                  protoporphyrin IX, rose Bengal                  (4,5,6,7-tetrachloro-2',4',5',7'-tetraiodofluorescein),                  erythrosin B, tris(bipyridine)ruthenium,                  riboflavin or Hemin. The light has a broad                  spectrum, including visible and near infrared                  wavelengths.
   ACTIVITY - Antibacterial.
   MECHANISM OF ACTION - None given.
   EXAMPLE - Freshly synthesized silver-silicon dioxide                nanoparticles were dispersed in 10 ml aqueous                solution under stirring. 10 ml Dimethylformamide                solution of 1 mM                tris(2,2'-bipyridyl)dichlororuthenium(II)                hexahydrate was added and stirred at room                temperature for 72 hours. The products was                centrifuged and washed to remove any unbound                photosensitizers to obtain silver-silicon                dioxide-tris(2,2'-bipyridyl)dichlororuthenium(ll)                hexahydrate.

   USE - The method is useful for preparing (I), which                is useful: as a solvent for synthesis of polymers;                and in extraction process used in petrochemical                industry.
   ADVANTAGE - The method is capable of preparing (I) without                requiring metal catalyst and without the problem of                sensitivity to oxidation.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Preparing lactam derivatives of formula (I),                comprises reacting an acid compound of formula                (CH3-C(=O)-C(R1)(R2)-C(R3)(R4)-C(=O)OH) (II) and a                primary amine compound of formula (RNH2) (III) in                the presence of at least a stoichiometric amount of                formic acid relative to the amine and in the                absence of metal catalyst.
   R=H, 1-25C-alkyl, 1-25C-aryl,                1-25C-heteroalkyl, 1-25C-heteroaryl, 1-25C-alkenyl                or linear or branched 1-25C-alkynyl;
   R1-R4=H or 1-8C-alkyl; and
   R5=group having 1-25 carbon atoms.
                                                                                                                     
TF TECHNOLOGY FOCUS - ORGANIC CHEMISTRY - Preferred Components: The reacting step is                  carried out in the absence of solvent at a                  pressure of 60 atmosphere (atm) and a temperature                  of 25-200&#176; C. The acid is 100%                  biobased-levulinic acid. The catalytic amount of                  formic acid is 100-200 mole%, based on the number                  of moles of primary amine (R-NH2). The primary                  amine and the acid are added in equimolar                  amount.
   Preferred Definitions:
   R=methyl, n-propyl, iso-propyl, n-octyl,                n-butyl, iso-butyl, tert-butyl benzyl or                cyclohexyl; and
   R5=methyl.
   SPECIFIC COMPOUNDS - 7 Compounds (I) are disclosed e.g.                  3-(2-methyl-5-oxo-pyrrolidin-1-yl)propyl of                  formula (Ia),                  4-(2-methyl-5-oxo-pyrrolidin-1-yl)butyl,                  1-(cyclohexylmethyl)-5-methyl-pyrrolidin-2-one,                  8-(2-methyl-5-oxo-pyrrolidin-1-yl)octyl, and                  5-methyl-1-phenethyl-pyrrolidin-2-one.
                                                                                                                                 
   EXAMPLE - 1 equivalent of Levulinic acid was charged to                an autoclave and then 1 molar equivalent of amine                was slowly added via a syringe and 1 molar                equivalent of formic acid was added. The autoclave                was closed once all the reagents were added. The                reaction was performed at 200&#176; C for 3-10                hours. The reaction mixture was cooled to room                temperature before degassing the autoclave and once                the reaction was completed, a solution with a brown                to orange color was obtained. The crude reaction                product was washed with diluted solution of sodium                carbonate and extracted with ethyl acetate,                depending on the nature of the amine used. The                separated organic phase was washed with brine,                dried with magnesium sulfate, filtered and                concentrated under reduced pressure to obtain                3-(2-methyl-5-oxo-pyrrolidin-1-yl)propyl.

AB    NOVELTY - A method (M1) for biosynthesizing at least one                product selected from adipic acid, 6-aminohexanoic                acid, hexamethylenediamine, caprolactam, and                1,6-hexanediol, involves enzymatically synthesizing                a 6C aliphatic backbone and enzymatically forming,                in at least one step, two terminal functional                groups selected from carboxyl, amine, and hydroxyl                groups in the backbone to directly produce the                product or producing the product in a subsequent                step.
   USE - For biosynthesizing at least one product                selected from adipic acid, 6-aminohexanoic acid,                hexamethylenediamine, caprolactam, and                1,6-hexanediol (claimed) used as intermediate in                the production of nylons such as nylon 6 and nylon                6,6.
   ADVANTAGE - The method efficiently combines appropriate                non-natural pathways, feedstocks, host                microorganisms, attenuation strategies to the                host's biochemical network, and cultivation                strategies to efficiently produce 6C building                blocks.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - An INDEPENDENT CLAIM is included for new                recombinant host comprising at least one exogenous                nucleic acid encoding at least one of a formate                dehydrogenase, enoyl-CoA reductase,                trans-2-enoyl-CoA hydratase, 3-hydroxybutyryl-CoA                dehydrogenase, acetyl-CoA C-acyltransferase,                acetoacyl-CoA reductase, acetyl-CoA synthetase,                acetyl-CoA carboxylase, a malic enzyme, puridine                nucleotide transhydrogenase,                glyceraldehyde-3P-dehydrogenase, acyl-CoA                hydrolase, aldehyde dehydrogenase, thioesterase,                cytochrome P450/&#969; -hydroxylase, alcohol                dehydrogenase, 6-hydroxyhexanoate dehydrogenase,                6-oxohexanoate dehydrogenase, diamine transaminase,                propionyl-CoA synthetase, and a carboxylate                reductase, where the host comprises at least one                deficiency in glucose-6-phosphate isomerase,                acetate kinase, an enzyme degrading pyruvate to                lactate, enzymes mediating the degradation of                phosphoenolpyruvate to succinate, alcohol                dehydrogenase, pyruvate decarboxylase, 2-oxoacid                decarboxylase, triose phosphate isomerase, a                glutamate dehydrogenase.

AB    NOVELTY - A catalyst (C1) comprising at least one                catalytically active element and at least one                helper catalyst, is new.
   USE - As a catalyst used in carbon dioxide (CO 2 )                sensor and fuel cell, and for making a chemical                reaction product comprising at least one of the                following: carbon monoxide (CO), hydroxy (OH - ),                aldehyde (HCO), formaldehyde (H 2 CO), HCO 2- ,                formic acid (H 2 CO 2 ), methanol (CH 3 OH),                methane (CH 4 ), ethylene (C 2 H 4 ), ethanol (CH 3                CH 2 OH), acetyl acetate (CH 3 COO), acetic acid                (CH 3 COOH), ethane (C 2 H 6 ), oxygen (O 2 ),                hydrogen (H 2 ), carboxylic acid ((COOH) 2 ), (COO                - ) 2 , chlorine (Cl 2 ), bromine (Br 2 ), iodine                (I 2 ), sodium hydroxide (NaOH), potassium                hydroxide (KOH), sodium hypochlorite (NaClO),                sodium chlorate (NaClO 3 ), potassium chlorate                (KClO 3 ), and trifluoroacetic acid (CF 3 COOH)                (claimed), and used in homogeneously or                heterogeneously catalyzed reactions, chemical                reactions in chemical plants, power plants, in                pollution control equipment and devices, chemical                reactions in fuel cells and in sensors.
   ADVANTAGE - The catalyst mixture can overcome the                limitations of low rates, high overpotentials and                low electron conversion efficiencies (namely,                selectivities) for catalytic reactions and high                power for sensors. The catalyst mixture includes at                least one catalytically active element, and at                least one helper catalyst. When the catalytically                active element and the helper catalyst are                combined, the rate and/or selectivity of a chemical                reaction can be enhanced over the rate seen in the                absence of the helper catalyst. For e.g. the                overpotential for electrochemical conversion of                carbon dioxide can be reduced, and the current                efficiency (namely, selectivity) for CO 2conversion                can be increased. The catalysts that include                catalytically active elements and helper catalysts                enhance the rate of a wide variety of chemical                reactions including: homogeneously catalyzed                reactions, heterogeneously catalyzed reactions,                chemical reactions in chemical plants, chemical                reactions in power plants, chemical reactions in                pollution control equipment and devices, chemical                reactions in fuel cells, and chemical reactions in                sensors.
   DETAILED DESCRIPTION - An INDEPENDENT CLAIM is included for making a                chemical reaction product, involving: combining a                catalytically active element and helper catalyst in                the presence of a reactant; allowing a reaction to                proceed to produce a reaction product.
